To add humour to humiliation, Krsna, raising both palms in the air in a show of contrition, said, “I confess! I cannot bear to see a faultless maidservant reprimanded for telling the truth.”

Calling the gopis to Him with a wave of His hand, Krsna went on to say, “Friends! If you gather around I will reveal how your saintly princess accosted Me on the road and forced Her affections on Me.”

Sti Radha gasped and, knitting Her eyebrows, playfully struck Krsna with a lotus flower. “You!” She loudly protested. “You who waylay gopis along even the royal road claim to have been accosted?”

Krsna’s accusation was so outrageous that eventually even Sri Radha laughed. Nonetheless, Visakha came to Her rescue, initiating a battle of wits, and wit, with Krsna that could bewilder goddess Sarasvati.

Having successfully silenced Krsna and sobered the gopis with her arguments, Visakha took the opportunity to glorify Radha’s and Krsna’s divine love by extolling Their pastimes. “Friends!" she began.

“Radha and Krsna are inseparable dual aspects of the Absolute Truth, appearing as a gopi and gopa in Vraja. He is famous because His name is linked to Her; She is famous because Her name is linked to Him.

“Driven by the anger of love, Radha sometimes conceals Her smile and refuses to speak to Krsna. At other times, fearing Cupid’s arrows, She embraces and takes shelter of happily smiling Hari.

“Sometimes jealous anger creates apparent enmity between that young couple and They refuse to speak to each other. Then one of you, or Cupid himself, intercedes, and They again smile and converse.

“Why do They argue? Her playful tricks and jokes sometimes anger Krsna, and He refuses to speak to Her until She apologises by suddenly and unexpectedly embracing Him with Her whole heart.

“At other times, Radha may hear from a mischievous friend that Krsna met Candravali on the paths of Vraja and went off to enjoy with her instead, which fills Radha with spite and jealous anger.

“You have all seen how Krsna, repentant, then places the red lac from Srimati Radharani’s soles on His head and repeatedly begs Her forgiveness, and how She gazes at Him with eyes full of hurt.”

Visakha concluded, “These exchanges are completely transcendental. They are the resonance between the divine lover and the divine beloved. Of the two, we consider Radha more important.

“We consider Radha more important because Her only desire is to see Krsna fully satisfied. Radha knows that He can be only be satisfied by Her, even when She hears that He has gone with some other gopi.

“Her jealous anger, then, is not anger at all. It is pure love, free from any selfish interest. She suffers only because She knows that Krsna is suffering without Her, even when He is with someone else.”

Upon hearing this conclusion, Krsna’s heart bubbled over with bliss. But in response to Visakha’s concluding statements He made some noises, as if objecting to being ranked second behind Radharani.

Understanding Krsna’s mind, Visakha said, “For example, our Syama is most famous for what?” “His lotus feet," one gopi exclaimed. “Correct!” Visakha replied. “But how have His feet become so sweet?

“What is the sweetness that makes baby-Krsna suck His own toes as other children do their thumbs? How is it that He spends His time during the universal dissolution drinking that same nectar?

“From where does that soft, sweet, radiant taste of Krsna’s feet come?” The gopis stared blankly at one another. They couldn’t reply because they didn’t know. But surely their tutor did.

Visakha answered her own question: “We should all carefully note this poem composed by Rati manjari,

*krsna-pusti-karamodi-sudha saradhikadhara*  
*sva-nadhuritva-sampadi krsna-padambujamrta*

**“‘Radha nourishes Krsna with the most sweet and fragrant nectar of Her lips, and that sweetness of Hers flows down throughout Krsna’s body and creates the famous nectar of His lotus feet.’”**

Visakha’s speech was followed by a moment of astonished silence and then a shower of unanimous applause. Radha covered Her face, and Rati, from sheer embarrassment, hid in the bushes nearby.

Finally, Lalita reminded everyone what their plan had been for the day. They still had a ways to go to reach Kosi, so Krsna and the gopis once again followed the road until they reached the tip of the dhama.

**\* \* \* \***

In this last leg of their procession Krsna did not walk ahead as the guide of a reserved group of pilgrims but strolled by Sri Radha’s side, flanked by uninhibited gopis who boldly sang of His love for Her.

The spiritual exhilaration of the gopis was rousing; indeed, the trees burst into bloom, the birds sang in full concert, and the forest animals followed the procession, singing and dancing in their own ways.

As they reached the boundary of Vrndavana-dhama, the gopis beheld a majestic banyan tree. An ornate swing hung trom one of the tree’s branches, begging to be used in service to the divine couple.

The surrounding area was rich with fruitbearing trees both pleasing to the eye and attractive to the tongue. Thinking this could be their final diversion before Kosi, the gopis said, “Please, let us stop here.”

The gopis request was music to Krsna’s ears. In Radha’s company He had no interest in attending Dvaraka-puja at Kosi. Careful not to upset Radha, however, He replied, “It’s up to your queen to decide.”

Radha was intoxicated by Krsna’s presence, by Their earlier pastimes together, and by the enthusiasm of Her friends. Throwing caution to the wind, She pointed to the banyan tree and said, “Let’s swing!”

The gopis deposited their items of worship safely nearby and then joined Radha and Krsna at the banyan tree, where the charming lotus-shaped swing swayed in the wind, eager to be of service.

Helping His beloved onto the swing, Krsna said, “Some time ago I came here with My friends. After decorating Me with the wealth of this forest, they enjoyed swinging with Me, one after another.”

Before Krsna could climb beside Radha, one gopi said, “Why don’t You decorate Radha as the gopas decorated You?” In the middle of mounting the swing, Krsna stopped and said, “Wonderful idea!”

Krsna stood before Radha, gently pushing Her swing, while His dark eyes eagerly swarmed about Her gently-smiling face like a pair of bumblebees engrossed in a golden lotus exuding honey.

The manjaris didn’t have to be told what to do: they fanned out in all directions to collect flowers, buds, leaves, and pigments from the forest while Vrnda emptied the contents of one of her storehouses.

Upon their return to the swing, the gopis were amused to find that Radha’s moving face on the swing had hypnotised Krsna, just as a shiny object swung by a magician hypnotises his subject.

What to do? Should they interrupt His ecstasy? Although the gopis were hesitating, Radha took charge. “Syama!” She said. “Will You decorate Me or not?” Krsna’s spell was broken.

The gopis stood in readiness, each holding trays with different items: flowers, perfumes, cosmetics, paints, ribbons, flower ornaments, and so on. Stirred by the mood of devotional service, Krsna set to work.

First He steadied the swing and then He refreshed the line of vermilion in the part of Radha’s hair. The gopis laughed: Radha’s paramour was re-applying the mark that signified Her marital status.

“Abhimanyu is in Krsna’s debt!" whispered one gopi to another. Then Krsna applied different cosmetics to Radharani’s face and framed His handiwork with tiny white, aromatic jasmine flowers.

He refreshed the drawings He had painted earlier, placed flower ornaments on Her head like a tiara, set earrings in their place, wove a flower garland in Her braid, and painted dolphins on Her chest.

Krsna used everything the gopis had brought, tastefully ornamenting Radha without overloading Her. He did everything just right, enhancing but never cluttering Her beauty. The gopis were amazed.

Fully ornamented, Srimati Radharani appeared like a floral goddess, like the quintessence of Krsna’s artistic skill, like the presiding deity of the gopis’ devotions—truly the Queen of Vrndavana.

Then , as before, Krsna stood in front of Srimati Radharani and gently pushed the swing, watching Her move towards Him and then away from Him, oblivious to anything or of anyone else.

Gone were Krsna’s pranks of swinging Radha to the treetops, or filling the swing with many sakhis and manjaris. He had carefully decorated Her. Now He was relishing His achievement.

To complement Krsna’s meditation the gopis took up their instruments and sang songs, relishing Radha’s and Krsna’s exchange hy watching Them and listening to Them praise each other.

The gopis sang: “We offer our respectful obeisances to the original source and supreme enjoyer of the amorous mellow, Sri Krsna, as with great pleasure He serves His own pleasure potency, goddess Radha.

“Intricately decorated with fresh forest flowers, Sri Radha swings in Krsna’s gaze, just as a golden lotus sparkling with diamond droplets of water sways in a gentle wind on the river Yamuna.

“One gopi claps her hands, another dances, and still another plays karatalas, while all the others sing the glories of the divine couple, who have filled Their hearts with the bliss of divine love.

“O Krsna, wearing a forest garland! ? Radha, beautified by the Lord’s own hands. ? divine couple who taste the nectar of love for each other. We, Your happy maidservants, offer our hearts to You.”

Because Sri Krsna decorated (srngara) Radharani beneath this banyan tree, it is known as Srngara vata. The area around it is known as Srngara, and the forest in this area, Srngaravana.

Devotees who want to learn the best way of dressing and ornamenting Radha and Syama may visit Srngara-vata with the hope of one day being able to see how Krsna Himself practises the art.

Oh yes! What happened to the worship at Kosi? Eventually Krsna and the gopis resumed their pilgrimage and arrived at Gomati-kunda, where, guided by brahmanas, they performed their prescribed rites.

Since the tinkling of Radha’s bracelets makes Krsna forget His own body, and since the sound of Krsna’s flute destroys Radha’s concern for family reputation, it’s no wonder They were late getting home.

Jatila was extremely angry when Radha finally arrived, but Visakha explained that their late return was due to their successful worship of Dvaraka, where time moves more slowly than in Vraja.

Thus another day of Radha’s and Krsna’s pastimes — which began with Their outing in Srngaravana — came to an end. My Parikrama of the eastern petal of New Vraja-dhama had also come to a close.

Fortunate persons whose hearts are full with feelings of attachment and who yearn to taste the nectar of service to Radha and Syama will happily visit the Lord’s pastime places in this part of the dhama.

I have described the prominent pastime places in this region in stanzas rich with the nectar-showering clouds of Lord Krsna’s holy names, unique attributes, and loving pastimes.

I am a sinful and fallen wretch. Bowing before the lotus feet of Radha and Syama, who are the goal of my life, I humbly pray, “May the divine couple one day mercifully engage me in Their service.”

*Let us worship the town known as Pañcakrośī Vṛndāvana which is replete with unique pastime places like Sevā-kuñja, where Kṛṣṇa became Rādhā’s menial servant.*

## Chapter 19 - Seva-kunja to Ramana-reti

I worship the forest of Bhauma Vrndavana, which is an ocean of pure love. Two divine aquatics constantly dive and surface and dive again in the waters of that ocean, performing unlimited blissful pastimes.

May Vrndavana forest, the essence of sweetness, which is manifesting its potencies in New Vraja-dhama, bless me with the kind of devotion needed to serve the enchanting young couple, Radha-Syamasundara.

I pray that my mind may become worthy to taste the nectar of seeing those two supremely enchanting youths — one fair and one dark — who enjoy transcendental pastimes in the forest of Vrndavana.

Why should I care for the four goals of life when I can live in Vrndavana forest in New Vraja-dhama and serve that divine couple, chanting Their names and remembering Tbeir pastimes?

I would rather serve the forest of Vrndavana, which itself serves Radha and Syama with picturesque beauty, aromatic flowers, tranquil waters, singing birds, and ideal places for transcendental pastimes.

Let me now glorify the heart of that divine forest — the central portion of New Vraja-dhama — which is the setting for innumerable confidential pastimes and which gives residence to a host of devotees.

Dear readers! May I remind you that the Parikrama of New Vraja-dhama I describe in this and the following three chapters does not correspond in detail to the Parikrama of Bhauma Vrndavana.

As already mentioned, to inspire the Krsna consciousness of New Vraja-dhama’s residents, their homes have been designated as the temples, samadhis, and pastime places in the town of Vrndavana.

Thus in describing the Vrndavana village in New Vraja-dhama, I make specific reference to the houses, roads, and other distinguishing features unique to this manifestation of Radha’s and Krsna’s home.

And although the details of the Parikrama in the two dhamas may sometimes differ, the narrations of the pastimes and the descriptions of the great devotees who are worshipped there remain the same.

My hope is that the inconvenience caused by the variances between the two parikramas is offset by the detail with which I have described the Lord’s pastimes. May the kind reader be pleased with me.

After visiting the pastime places in the eastern portion of New Vraja-dhama, I left Kamaravana and walked back down to the Parikrama path and headed towards Radha-Syama’s temple and Nimavana.

From the tip of Nimavana I always revel in seeing Nidhuvana, descending as it docs in a series of terraced gardens to Visakha-kunda, and beyond that to Nikunjavana and Syamananda Prabhu’s samadhi.

At night I often stand here looking at Nidhuvana, Vrndavana beyond that, and beyond that, the tree-line silhouetted against the night sky, softly lit by the reflected glow of the distant town of Kaposvar.

Nidhuvana, with its pavilion, lake, and gardens, was sponsored by a devotee in the memory of bis deceased father. The donor will benefit from his charity by entering the eternal abode of the Lord.

Scriptures declare that those who establish such pastime places, or finance the construction of a temple for Sri Krsna, achieve benefits envied by yogis, sages, and even practising devotees.

Merely thinking of constructing a temple tor the Lord frees one from the sins of a hundred births, and planning it, a thousand births, while actually building it grants any perfection the benefactor desires.

This means that the sponsor of such a temple can attain the heavenly planets, liberation, the abode of Lord Visnu, even Goloka Vrndavana, depending upon the strength and purity of the sponsor’s desire.

As wonderful as such benefits may seem, they arc multiplied three times over when gardens, pavilions, or temples are established at holy places like Bhauma Vrndavana or New Vraja-dhama.

The act of erecting an abode for the Deity of the Lord is so transcendentally potent that even a child who builds a temple from mud, leaves, or twigs during play quickly attains Sri Krsna’s abode.

The act of constructing a temple or pastime place offers such inconceivable benefits because these devotional deeds please the Lord and give shelter to the residents of the three created worlds.

That is to say, because the universe is contained within the Deity of Krsna, and because His temple is equal to Vaikuntha, building a temple brings Vaikuntha to all the suffering souls of this world.

Even if the people of this age do not take direct shelter of Krsna’s temples by visiting or worshipping there, they get relief from a certain degree of distress merely because such temples exist in the world.

Many sincere people from the UK and Hungary supported the effort to construct Radha’s and Syamasundara’s existing temple. Amongst them, one devotee in Budapest was the primary donor.

Sometimes I would fret, not knowing where the money for the next day’s expenses would come from. He would always reassure me, “Don’t worry. Just build the temple, and I will take care of the hills.”

Scriptures confirm that this devotee, having used his wealth in the best possible way, pleased Radha-Syamasundara. He will surely receive Their full mercy at the end of his life.

Scriptures also declare that prosperous Vaisnavas who know their duty in this regard, hut neglect doing the needful, squander their hard-earned piety and lose not only their wealth but also their homes.

Yamaraja once told his messengers: “O servants! You should not bring to me those who have built a temple of the Lord and served the Deity, but those who are capable but neglect to do so."

As soon as we completed the existing temple of Radha-Syamasundara, we drew up plans for a permanent Vedic-style temple that was supposed to be fully constructed within twenty years.

Since then we have many times modified that model, but the plan to construct an edifice worthy of Their Lordships is still alive. My hope is that, in the future, devotees of means will realise this goal.

My ambition is, before I die, to see Radha-Syamasundara housed in a temple that reflects Their glory, attracts unlimited guests to Their lotus feet, and provides for the needs of New Vraja-dhama’s residents.

Until then, I will continue to pray that one day I might see Radha and Syamasundara with these very eyes as I daily see Them in my mind’s eye while I offer this prayer:

“In a temple of jewels in Vrndavana, underneath a desire tree, Sri Sri Radha-Govinda, served by Their most confidential associates, sit upon an effulgent throne. I offer my humble obeisances unto Them.”

**\* \* \* \***

Nidhuvana is the first pastime place on the parikrama of Vrndavana town. Sukadeva Gosvami made Nidhuvana famous by revealing that it was the place where Krsna took Radha before the rasa dance.

At the beginning of the dance the gopis became a little proud that Krsna had shown them so much attention. Each of them thought, “Of all women, I must be the most attractive to Krsna.”

Because Krsna does not like to see pride in His devotees, and because He wanted to show the gopis that Srimati Radharani was actually the most attractive to Him, He disappeared with Her from their midst.

While the gopis searched after Krsna, the divine couple enjoyed a variety of loving diversions in the groves of this forest, for which it is known as Nidhuvana (“the forest of loving pastimes”).

Acaryas like Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami also tell us that Radha and Krsna regularly meet in Nidhuvana at night, and at i hose times that They feel tired, They rest there in a secluded grove.

The jewelled pavilion named Rang-mahal, a favourite resting place of the divine couple, is found in that grove. A pavilion in New Vraja-dhama and a temple in Bhauma Vrndavana commemorate that place.

The first time I saw Nidhuvana in Bhauma Vrndavana from atop the surrounding wall, I was struck by the beauty and antiquity of the gnarled, bower-like trees, each appearing like a grove unto itself.

Here in New Vraja-dhama we carefully selected and planted special trees, hoping to mirror the effect of Nidhuvana in Bhauma Vrndavana, and to give Radha-Syama similar pleasure groves.

When dawn approaches, Vrnda-devi inspires her parrots to wake the divine couple. The gopis then dress and ornament Them and offer mangala-arati to remove all obstacles to Their next meeting.

During this morning period, Krsna sometimes decorates Srimati Radharani, braiding Her hair and applying various cosmetics, just as He sometimes does before the night-time rasa dance.

Once the gopis went with Radha and Krsna to Nidhuvana. When Visakha complained of being thirsty, Krsna poked the ground with His flute and created a pond, now called Visakha-kunda.

Visvanatha Cakravarti Thakura writes that Radha once woke Krsna in Nidhuvana to tell Him about a dream She had just had, a dream that had both enchanted Her heart and baffled Her mind.

Radha said, “I just had the most unusual dream! A golden-complexioned brahmana was dancing and singing in the midst of a multitude of people along the bank of a river.

“I could not recognise Him or the people or their music or the place, but they all still seemed extremely familiar; indeed, I was reminded of Our rasa dance by the river Yamuna’s shore.”

Krsna sat up, appearing both amused and intrigued. He asked, “Tell Me more. Who was that golden youth You saw?” Radha replied, “That’s just it. I don’t know. His behaviour confused Me.

“He displayed symptoms of intense ecstasy. I felt drawn to Him. And because He sometimes cried out, ‘O Radha! ? Radha’, I thought He could only be You.” Krsna prompted Her, “Then?”

Catching Her breath, Radha went on, “But the next moment He started to call out, ‘O Krsna! ? Krsna!’ just as I call out for You. I was forced to wonder if the youth could be Me.

“Now I am confused. If that brahmana was You, then where was I? And if He was Me, then where were You?” Krsna took Radha’s hand and started to tease Her, “O Kutukini, My curious one!

“In the past You have seen so many of My incarnations — Narayana, Varaha, and Nrsimha — without being confused. It really is puzzling that You can’t identify this brahmana. I wonder who He is!”

Krsna paused, leaned back on His pillow, and pretended to be thoughtfully considering what Radha had said. Suddenly Radha saw Her dream unfolding within the Kaustubha gem on Krsna’s chest.

Now She could understand that the youth was indeed Krsna. She supported Her conclusion by citing Garga Muni’s prediction that in the future Krsna would appear in a golden form.

“But,” She concluded, “since You did not tell Me that this brahmana was You, as You had done with Your other incarnations, and since He called out Your name, I think there must be more to His identity.

“I think the brahmana is both You and Me.” Excited, Radha went on, “Incredible! Just as I uttered those words, My heart confirmed that they are true. I am no longer perplexed. I now understand My dream.”

Radha and Krsna then talked about Their combined appearance as Lord Caitanya and His blessings on humankind until Their girlfriends joined Them in the grove surrounding Rang-mahal.

In a corner of Nidhuvana — which overlaps adjoining Nikunjavana — is a grove in which the gopis once stole Krsna’s flute and demanded, in jest, that He take music lessons.

At first Krsna couldn’t tolerate being accused of hitting wrong notes, but Radha gave a beautiful flute recital that won a standing ovation from Lalita and Visakha, and made Krsna admit His musical faults.

We are used to seeing the flute in Krsna’s hands. Rarely do we hear of or see Radha playing the flute. But by remembrance in nama-sankirtana we can relish the vision of flute-playing Radha.

During the time of the Gosvamis, a Vaisnava saint and musician named Svami Haridasa lived in Nidhuvana, where he worshipped the divine couple by composing beautiful devotional songs.

One day Krsna appeared to him in the form of a Deity, His body handsomely bent in three places. Some say that the Deity appeared near Rang-mahal, others say that He was found in Visakha-kunda.

Haridasa was eager to worship Sri Radha. So to confirm that both Radha and Krsna are present in that form, the Deity’s right leg is the leg of His consort. Haridasa named the Deity Kunja-bihari.

After Haridasa passed away, the Deity became known as Banke-bihari, now the most popular Deity in Vrndavana. On the Aksaya-trtiya in the month of Vaisakha, the Lord’s legs are made visible.

The Deity of Banke-bihari appeared near Rang-mahal. To the far side of Nidhuvana is the samadhi of Svami Haridasa as well as a temple of Srimati Radharani where She taught Krsna flute lessons.

Oral tradition has it that Haridasa was the guru of Tansena, the famous musician, jewel of Emperor Akbar’s court. It is said that Akbar once came to Nidhuvana with Tansena to hear Haridasa sing.

Dressed as a commoner, Emperor Akbar watched and listened with joy as Tansena first performed a song for his guru in the popular dhrupada-raga, accompanying himself with his vina.

When his student had finished, Haridasa took the vina and pointed out Tansena’s mistakes. Haridasa then sang the same raga so sweetly that all the birds and animals in the nearby forest gathered to hear.

Akbar had never heard such beautiful singing, nor had he heard a devotional performance that could attract all living entities. He praised the saint, but left without disclosing his identity.

From the effect Svami Haridasa’s singing had on a materialistic king, as well as the creatures of the forest, we can just imagine the effects Krsna’s flute playing has on His pure devotees and eternal associates.

What can be said, then, about how the songs of the cowherd boys and girls affect Krsna? Their songs express the highest forms of love in a style most favoured by their beloved and most common to Goloka.

Sri Radha’s dream revealed that Caitanya Mahaprabhu and His associates bring to earth that eternal music festival of love, with all its spiritual treasures, in the form of the sankirtana movement.

One devotee praises Sri Caitanya’s meditation on the divine couple’s Nidhuvana pastimes,

*radha-krsna eka tanu hai nidhuvana-majhe bamsi bajay*  
*visvarupa ki prabhuji sahi aota prakatahi nadiya men*

**“To experience the utmost intimacy, Radha and Krsna united in one form within a grove of Nidhuvana and played the flute. Adopting that form and mood, the Lord of Visvarupa has appeared in Nadia.”**

**\* \* \* \***

From Nidhuvana I followed the Parikrama path around the forest of Nikunjavana, which contains Seva-kunja, and stood facing Radha-Syama’s temple, with Rupa Gosvami’s nama-samadhi at my back.

In the evenings I walk with some devotees along this path from the temple and offer my respects to the pastime places along the way. On clear nights I invariably stop to look up at the star-lit sky.

When the moon is up, the beauty of the night, the sky, and New Vraja-dhama increases, and I look up wondering whether the devas are gathering to witness another nocturnal pastime of Radha-Syama.

The road approaches the northern tips of both Nidhuvana and Seva- kunja forming four paths that Radha and the gopis walk. These paths represent Kunja-gali, Dana-gali, Gumana-gali, and Mana-gali.

Kunja-gali is the Parikrama path from Yavata. The gopis take it to come to Seva-kunja. Dana-gali is the path running along the side of Nidhuvana. Krsna once asked Radha for a toll to pass through there.

The portion of the Parikrama path in front of my cottage down to the intersection is called Gumana-gali. It was there that Radha completely ignored Krsna’s demands and avoided Him.

Finally, a path runs along the western side of Seva-kunja in the direction of Ramana-reti. This path is called Mana-gali because Radha once used it when She left in a huff for Mana-sarovara.

The gopis followed Krsna’s footprints to find Him after He had disappeared with Radha. But when they came too close to the divine couple, Krsna disappeared, leaving Sri Radha behind.

After the gopis made Krsna re-appear by the strength of their pure, devotional songs, they then commenced the long-awaited rasa dance in the forest of Nikunjavana — more popularly known as Seva-kunja.

Ever since I moved into my cottage in 1998, I have been looking across our Seva-kunja and Nidhuvana at the temple, whether working at my desk, standing by the window, or sitting in the garden.

From the time they were merely hillsides, they have been objects of my vision and meditation, both day and night. Looking at them first inspired me to wonder what Radha and Syama might do there.

These spaces seemed suited to pay homage to Krsna’s rasa dance pastimes; in fact, when I actually began to map out New Vraja-dhama, the two hillsides did correspond to Seva-kunja and Nidhuvana.

On my first visit to Bhauma Vrndavana, I was highly impressed with Seva-kunja, especially by the testimony of our guide that Radha and Krsna still spend the entire night there in divine revelry.

There is a small temple — another Rang-mahal — where Krsna served Radha, a very deep lake called Lalita-kunda, and the path of the rasa dance, which snakes through the entire area.

It is well known that any unqualified person who dares spend the night in Seva-kunja will either die or go insane, a reaction devotees need not worry about in the Seva-kunja of New Vraja-dhama.

Fortunate devotees will he able to see and hear the dance festival of singing gopis, clapping hands, clashing batons, and the all-pervasive flute-song emanating from the swirling cloud of Krsna’s dancing.

“All glories to the place where the rasa dance of Sri Krsna was performed. All glories to Radha and Syama! All glories to the divine rasa dance, the most beautiful of all Lord Krsna’s pastimes.”

Once, during a lull in the dancing, Lalita-devi told Krsna that she was thirsty. No well was available and the Yamuna was far away, but she and the other girls were in dire need of refreshment.

To satisfy Lalita, Krsna dug His heel into the ground. From that depression a fountain of nectar bubbled up and quickly formed a little pond from which the girls could drink to their full satisfaction.

Krsna’s creating either a well or a kunda for His thirsty companions happens again and again in His Vrndavana pastimes, as a result of which such bodies of water are found all over Vraja.

It is well known that Vrnda always keeps these areas well stocked with refreshments, which may cause one to wonder why Lalita in Seva-kunja or Visakha in Nidhuvana would need to ask for water.

The main pastime places of Vrndavana all have storehouses filled with drinks, snacks, clothes, ornaments, and every other item needed for the service and worship of Krsna and His loved ones.

Every day, in the early morning, Paurnamasi comes to Vrnda’s residence near Nandagrama to tell her where Radha’s and Krsna’s pastimes will take place that day so that Vrnda can prepare.

Knowing that such an important event, attended by so many gopis, would take place on the full moon night of Karttika, Vrnda surely must have had plenty of cool water on hand for everyone.

And the senior gopis were well aware of this. Why, then, would they need to ask Krsna for what was already available? They wanted to give Krsna one more opportunity to reciprocate their love for Him.

These gopis were so moved, having spent the evening exchanging love with Krsna, that they were impelled hy feelings of possessiveness to request that Krsna show His love by giving them water.

On the night of the saradiya-rasa dance, Lalita was not the only gopi to request something personal of Krsna. By the end of the festival, Radha looked dishevelled. Of course, She could not he neglected.

Attended to hy Krsna and Her closest friends, Srimati Radharani nearly collapsed from exhaustion on a daybed in the beautifully formed and decorated grove at the centre of a forest garden.

As Krsna watched anxiously, Sri Radha’s maidservants wiped Her limbs with damp cloths, gave Her assorted refreshments, and fanned Her to remove the heat from Her body.

Radha knew that Krsna was eager to render some menial service for Her hut that He felt Himself unqualified, and so He was hesitant to ask. Taking the dominant role in love, Sri Radha said:

“Syama! Would You like to help? My clothes are in disarray, My braid has opened, and the lacquer on My feet has worn away. If You like, You may assist Me by putting these things in order.”

Krsna needed no more encouragement than that. At once He knelt before Radha, and as Her maidservants handed Him what His service required, He personally served Radha to His heart’s content.

Krsna finds even greater pleasure in serving His devotees than they do in serving Him. Just as Krsna took the role of Arjuna’s charioteer, He now took the role of Radharani’s menial servant.

Krsna dressed and ornamented Radha, applied sandalwood paste to Her limbs, and braided delicate flower garlands into Her hair. Before painting Her feet, He tenderly massaged them.

Krsna thought that Radha’s rose-petal feet must have become tired and sore from so much dancing. To relieve Her discomfort, He gently rubbed Her soles and then covered them with red lacquer.

Radha watched the intense expressions on Krsna’s face. The manjaris, eager to learn, carefully took note of His techniques. And Radha’s sakhis relished the nuances of Radha’s and Krsna’s loving exchange.

In this grove, Krsna rendered the most menial service (seva) to Sri Radha, and so the gopis at once decided to call it Seva-kunja. But, in time, the entire forest of Kunjavana was identified by that name.

**\* \* \* \***

In the northwest corner of New Vraja-dhama’s Seva-kunja is a house in which devotees worship a picture of Syama as Radha-ramana, the one who gives pleasure to Sri Radha by His submissive service to Her.

This house represents Bhauma Vrndavana’s temple of Radha-ramana, the Deity who kindly appeared to Gopala Bhatta Gosvami. Let us now briefly praise Radha-ramana, who is honoured here.

When they met at Sri Ranga-ksetra, Caitanya Mahaprabhu ordered Gopala Bhatta to go to Vrndavana, where the young sannyasi was welcomed by Rupa Gosvami and Sanatana Gosvami as their younger brother.

News that Gopala Bhatta had arrived in Vraja reached Lord Caitanya in Puri, and the Lord sent a letter to His devotee, along with a set of His kaupinas, an outer cloth, and a cauki on which the Lord had sat.

Rupa and Sanatana explained to Gopala Bhatta that, by sending the cauki, Lord Caitanya had authorised him to initiate disciples. He accepted that service and performed it while sitting on the cauki.

The Lord had also instructed Gopala Bhatta to worship a Salagrama-sila. The Gosvami personally collected salagramas from the Himalayas and worshipped Them in a hut near Nidhuvana.

Gopala Bhatta kept the silas in a wicker basket that he hung from a tree. He would swing Them, hoping that the Lord would appear to him in a form like those worshipped by the other Gosvamis.

In His mind’s eye, Gopala Bhatta envisioned His worshipable Lord with the combined features of three Deities: a face like Govindadji’s, a chest like Gopinathah, and feet like Madana-mohana’s.

On the full-moon night of Vaisakha in the year 1542, Radha-ramana appeared directly of His own volition from one of the Salagrama-silas, looking exactly as Gopala Bhatta Gosvami had conceived of Him.

Radha-ramana is truly a wonderful Deity. Although small, only two hands high, He has all the anatomical features of a person, down to fingernails and ear holes. He is Krsna in every detail.

Of all the Deities of Vrndavana, Radha-ramana has remained there the longest, even during the iconoclastic raids of Aurangzeb, when most other Deities were moved to Jaipur or other safe havens.

Many incredible loving exchanges took place between Gopala Bhatta Gosvami and Radha-ramana. Once, the Deity called His servant and revealed Himself as Gopala, tending cows and surrounded by *gopas.*

On another occasion, Gopala Bhatta was feeling great separation from Caitanya Mahaprabhu, and so Radha-ramana manifested Himself as Gauranga in the freshness of youth, dressed as a brahmana.

For a moment Gopala Bhatta just stood and stared at the enchanting form of Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Then, overcome with bliss, he called out, “What have I seen!” and fell to the ground unconscious.

The author of *Bhakti-ratnakara* says,

**“Radha-ramana would at once satisfy whatever desire found a place in the heart of Gopala Bhatta.”**

Such is the bond between the Lord and His devotee.

Prior to the construction of His temple, Radha-ramana resided for many years in a very humble, rustic abode, much the same way as the other Gosvamis’ Deities had done — in a tree.

There used to he a large lake near Nidhuvana connected to the river Yamuna. During the day Radha-ramana’s servants would worship Him in a tree by the shore of that lake.

At night the Lord would he put to rest on a boat anchored in the middle of the lake. Guards with poison-tipped arrows would then stand watch, lest anyone tried to steal or harm Radha-ramana.

Srila Prabhupada considered the standard of worship offered to Radha-ramana to be excellent. Behind the Lord’s temple arc the *samadhi* of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami and the place the Deity appeared.

The Sanskrit work *Sadhana-dipika* describes the appearance of Radha-ramana:

**“I worship Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, whose life and soul were the lotus feet of Sri Govindadeva of Vrndavana —**

**“But who accepted the task of serving the Lord separately according to the instruction of Sri Rupa Gosvami in the beautiful form of Sri Radha-ramanadeva, who manifest Himself from a** ***salagrama-sila.***

**“Radha-ramana was not different from Govindadji, who appeared in response to Rupa Gosvami’s love; the reason for Gopala Bhatta being awarded His separate service can he known from reliable persons.**

**“I worship that inhabitant of Vrndavana, Sri Gopala Bhatta, son of Sri Venkata Bhatta, who was the elder brother of Tridandi Svami Srila Prabodhananda Sarasvati.”**

In front of Radha-ramana’s temple in New Vraja-dhama is a well that marks Sapta-samudrika, established when Indra’s charioteer brought water from the seven seas to bathe Krsna after He lifted Govardhana.

Whoever tries to grow vegetables, fruit, or especially grain in or near the area surrounding this house will always have an abundant yield, for this area corresponds to the residence of Annapurna-devi.

When Yogamaya was horn as Krsna’s sister, Kamsa attempted to kill her. She slipped from His hand, berated him, and then disappeared to Varanasi, where she is worshipped as Annapurna.

But Varanasi is also present in Vrndavana, and because Paurnamasi Yogamaya always resides in this corner of Seva-kunja, Annapurna, the goddess of grains, or nourishment, also resides there.

In New Vraja-dhama, Paurnamasi’s sitting place corresponds to the fruit orchard in the corner of Seva-kunja, from where she observes the different pastimes the divine couple enact in this lovely forest.

The last pastime place I will mention in Seva-kunja is the residence of Advaita Acarya, which is below the Advaita-vata tree in the small forest of Kisoravana just opposite the entrance to my cottage.

Advaita Acarya stayed there and saw the beauty of Seva-kunja during His visit to Vrndavana. From there He visited Gokula, found the Deity of Madana-gopala, and brought Him hack to Kisoravana.

Once, while Advaita Acarya was away on an errand, the Muslim king’s soldiers, having heard that a Deity was being worshipped here, came to Seva-kunja to destroy Madana-gopala.

With no one to protect Him, Gopala jumped off the altar and hid in the bushes while the soldiers searched the compound. Unable to find a Deity to desecrate, they finally left, unsuccessful.

Soon Advaita returned and saw that Madana-gopala was not on the altar. After a frantic search, He finally found the Lord hiding in the bushes. Seeing Advaita, the Deity jumped onto His lap, crying.

Madana-gopala was shaking in fear, but Advaita pacified Him. The Lord then told Him everything that had happened. To avoid keeping the Lord in such a remote place, Advaita took Him to Mathura.

Advaita Acarya put Madana-gopala in the care of a brahmana family in Mathura. The Lord stayed there until Sanatana Gosvami came to Vrndavana and took over the care of the Deity.

It is also said that Advaita Acarya found a picture of Krsna that Visakha-devi had drawn. Advaita is supposed to have worshipped it at this place, but the whereabouts of the picture are no longer known.

About Advaita Acarya it is said,

*advaitam harmadvaitad acaryam bhakti-samsanat*  
*bhaktavataram isam tam advaitacaryam asraye*

**“Because He is nondifferent from Hari, the Supreme Lord —**

**“He is called Advaita, and because He propagates the cult of devotion, He is called Acarya. He is the Lord and the incarnation of the Lord’s devotee. Therefore I take shelter of Him.”**

**\* \* \* \***

Iturned around to offer respects to my own place of worship, which represents both Radha-Damodara’s residence and Srila Prabhupada’s place of bhajana. I am blessed to live here.

North of Radha-Damodara’s temple, in the house next door, is the samadhi of Srila Rupa Gosvami Prabhupada. The disappearance of this Gaudiya Vaisnava acarya should always be celebrated there.

Because many of the homes in New Vraja-dhama are designated as the samadhis of Vaisnava acaryas, the truths and worship of samadhis arc described in the appendix “The Worship of Vaisnava Samadhis. ”

The Radha-Damodara temple in Bhauma Vrndavana is famous. In a compound beside the temple there are, in addition to Rupa Gosvami’s samadhi and bhajana-kutira, many other samadhis of great Vaisnavas.

Bhugarbha Gosvami, Jiva Gosvami, Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami, Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura, and King Virahamvira are some of these saints. Srila Prahhupada’s residential quarters are also there.

On the order of Caitanya Mahaprabhu, Rupa Gosvami came to Vrndavana in 1517 to discover the lost places of Krsna’s pastimes and to write books about devotional service to Radha and Krsna.

Sri Rupa lived at Dhruva-ghata until Sanatana Gosvami, carrying the same orders from Lord Caitanya, joined him some time later. The two brothers then took up residence in what is now Vrndavana village.

Rupa Gosvami lived and worshipped in other parts of Vraja, such as Terh-kadamba and Varsana, but the Radha-Damodara temple established by Jiva Gosvami was always Sri Rupa’s headquarters.

It is said that in the year 1564, a few years after the accession of Akbar to the throne, Rupa Gosvami left this world. His only disciple, Jiva Gosvami, oversaw the construction of Sri Rupa’s samadhi.

I request the residents of this house to serve a picture of Rupa Gosvami and maintain a simple shrine commemorating his passing so that Krsna Valley’s residents can come here for spiritual inspiration.

When Srila Prabhupada came to Vrndavana, he first lived in the Vamsi-gopala temple near Kesi-ghata, but he would regularly visit the Radha-Damodara temple, as was the custom of Sri R?pa’s followers.

In a dream Srila Prabhupada once saw many Vaisnavas chanting around Rupa Gosvami, who was sitting in the courtyard of his samadhi and looking at Srila Prabhupada in a very affectionate way.

Srila Prabhupada accepted the dream as a call from Rupa Gosvami and moved to the Radha-Damodara temple. He lived on the first floor until his permanent rooms on the ground floor were renovated.

Many years ago I went on Parikrama of Vrndavana with Gurudasa, one of Srila Prahhupada’s early disciples. At the Radha-Damodara temple he narrated a story Prabhupada had personally told him.

Srila Prabhupada said that he would always meditate upon Rupa Gosvami while taking his meals because he could see Sri Rupa’s samadhi through the small window of his kitchen-cum-dining room.

His Divine Grace also said that he would often sit in front of Rupa Gosvami,s samadhi and pray, “O Prabhupada! Please show yourself to me and give me guidance and strength to realise my mission.”

One day Rupa Gosvami did appear to Srila Prabhupada. Sri Rupa told him, “Now you have written some books. Take them to the western countries and preach. Do not hesitate any longer.”

This was the same instruction that Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati had given him decades earlier. Feeling fortified by Rupa Gosvami’s blessing, Srila Prabhupada finally decided to go to the West.

Rupa Gosvami, the founder of Lord Caitanya’s mission, had spoken directly to Srila Prabhupada the same message he had repeatedly spoken through his representative, Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati.

Let us offer a prayer to Rupa Gosvami for the strength to play our own roles in Krsna’s mission,

*sri-caitanya-mano-’bhistam sthapitam yena bhu-tale*  
*svayam rupah kada mahyam dadati sva-padantikam*

**“When will Srila Rupa Gosvami Prabhupada, who has established within this material world the mission to fulfil the desire of Lord Caitanya, give me shelter under his lotus feet?”**

Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, Sri Rupa’s close associate, composed a prayer in the same vein,

*adadanas trnam dantair idam yace punah punah*  
*srimad-rupa-padambhoja-rajo ’ham syam bhave bhave*

**“Taking a blade of grass between my teeth and falling to the ground like a stick, I beg again and again that I may obtain the dust of Srila Rupa Gosvamis lotus feet birth after birth.”**

Between the two buildings and just next to the Parikrama path is a well that represents the one commissioned by Rupa Gosvami and used to serve Radha-Damodara until Srila Prabhupada’s time.

Wells in Bhauma Vrndavana are holy because they are manifestations of Mother Earth’s direct service to the Lord. Others that were dug by the Lord Himself or filled with sacred waters are more hallowed still.

At the northeastern end of the garden is Harayola, which comprises a tranquil Krsna-kunda, a gaming room-like grove for the divine couple, and another grove for resting called Nikunja-mandira.

On the bank of this Krsna-kunda, Radha and Krsna play a variety of table games, such as dice. Radha once trounced Krsna so soundly at dice that Krsna was completely humiliated and downcast.

To cheer Krsna up, Lalita said, “Radha! You defeated Syama at tossing dice, but let’s see who wins at a game of loving pastimes.” At this the gopis jokingly nudged Radha to nearby Nikunja-mandira.

In Krsna’s manifest pastimes, He fully displayed His personal attributes at Seva-kunja, and so devotees consider it to be the best of all places in both the manifest and unmanifest realms.

Srila Prabhupada told his disciples that his quarters at the temple of Radha-Damodara in Bhauma Vrndavana is the hub of the wheel of the spiritual world. He also said that he resides there eternally.

Years ago, on the last day of a visit to Bhauma Vrndavana, I stayed in Srila Prabhupada’s rooms at the Radha-Damodara temple, chanting in his bedroom until late at night and then resting in the kitchen.

At that time I could perceive the truth that His Divine Grace lived there eternally. Recalling his daily schedule, I rose early, bathed in the aforementioned well, and attended mandala-arati.

I have no devotional credits, and yet Srila Prabhupada accepted me as his servant, drew me into the divine abode of Vraja, and blessed me with service to Radha-Syamasundara and Radha-Damodara.

Just writing such a testament to his causeless mercy fills me with wonder. But even more wonderful is the fact that Srila Prabhupada created a society to deliver that very same mercy to the whole world.

Anyone who turns away from Srila Prabhupada’s benediction moon to chase shooting stars instead is certainly a fool, for as His Divine Grace writes, “Only fools are ungrateful to their benefactors.”

I have mostly explained Srila Prabhupada’s mercy to me in the form of service to Radha-Syamasundara. Nonetheless, His Divine Grace also blessed me with the service of Radha-Damodara.

These same Deities of Radha-Damodara were the object of Svarupa Damodara Gosvamis personal worship during the time he served as Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s secretary in Jagannatha Puri.

In the early days of the Krsna consciousness movement, Srila Prabhupada taught us to worship Radha and Damodara hy offering ghee lamps and prayers every day during the month of Karttika.

This strict daily worship constitutes our main observance of urja-vrata during that holiest of months, a month that multiplies the result of our devotional practices one thousand times.

Dear readers! I shall now share with you a secret about Radha-Damodara in the hopes that it will increase your faith in Them, and in the glories of New Vraja-dhama and its holy places, of which I sing.

Whereas a Deity of Krsna would normally hold the flute to His right side, Damodara holds it to His left. From the moment I first saw Radha-Damodara in 1980, this unique detail captivated me.

Fifteen years later, I asked Their pujari how it was that Damodara held His flute as He does — obviously offering it to Srimati Radharani. Was He purposely fashioned in that extraordinary pose?

The pujari told me that Damodara had originally held the flute as Krsna normally does, but some generations after Svarupa Damodara’s departure, Radha asked Him for the flute and He moved His arms.

Radha-Damodara’s pose, then, reveals a pastime enacted at the tip of Nidhuvana — Radha teaching Krsna to play the flute. Now please hear something that I consider more than just coincidence.

From Their altar, Radha-Damodara now look directly out onto the grove where Radha gave Krsna flute lessons. And They appeared here years after I assigned the pastime places in New Vraja-dhama.

All glories, all glories to Radha and Damodara, the saviours of this fallen soul. By coming into my room, and my heart, They answered a song I had sung to Srimati Radharani and Her friends for years.

That famous prayer is known and sung by many a Vaisnava: “When, following the orders of Lalita, Visakha, and the other gopis, will I serve the lotus feet of the divine couple?”

Lalita-devi takes the form of Svarupa Damodara Gosvami in Lord Caitanya’s pastimes. I accept the appearance of his personal Deities into my life as his order to worship Them.

And since Radha-Damodara were Srila Prabhupada’s worshipable Deities, and Their temple is his eternal place of residence, I accept that order to have come through my divine spiritual master.

Srila Prabhupada taught us to pray,

*namas te stu damne sphurad-dipti dhamne tvadiyodarayatha visvasya dhamne*  
*namo radhikayai tvadiya-priyayai namo ’nanta-lilaya devaya tubhyam*

**“O Lord Damodara! I first of all offer my obeisances to the brilliantly effulgent rope that binds Your belly. I then offer my obeisances to Your belly, which is the abode of the entire universe.**

**“Again and again I humbly bow down to Your most beloved Srimati Radharani, and I offer all obeisances to You, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, who displays unlimited pastimes.”**

**\* \* \* \***

Rupa Gosvami accepted Jiva Gosvami as his only initiated disciple In 1542, during the month of Karttika, Rupa personally designed, carved, and then offered the Deity of Damodara to Jiva.

Jiva Gosvami first worshipped that Deity, much like other Vaisnavas worshipped their own Deities, in a grove of Seva-kunja. Later he erected a temple to provide better facilities for Their Lordships.

Yet it was only after Sanatana Gosvami passed away that Jiva took steps to permanently arrange for Lord Damodara’s worship hy purchasing the entire area of Seva-kunja for thirty-two rupees.

At that time Seva-kunja was much larger than it is now; indeed, just as it had during Krsna’s manifest presence, the area of Seva-kunja also encompassed Dana-gali, Imli-tala, and Vamsivata.

Radha-Damodara’s original temple was a humble one, but it was famous for housing a large library of ancient manuscripts and original works of the Gosvamis, including those of Rupa and Sanatana.

To serve New Vraja-dhama and its residents, I am also assembling in Radha-Damodara’s temple an extensive library of Vaisnava texts, both in their original languages and in English translations.

Oral tradition and written records describe many loving exchanges between Radha-Damodara and Jiva Gosvami, exchanges that always kept both the Lord and His devotee in ecstatic bliss.

Sometimes Radha and Damodara would request Jiva Gosvami to cook something in addition to Their regular offerings. After fulfilling Their Lordships’ desire, Jiva Gosvami would then watch Them eat.

Once, Damodara called Sri Jiva to come and hear His flute playing. When the Gosvami arrived, He saw the Lord in a youthful form, decorated with ornaments, playing an exceedingly lovely tune.

Seeing this remarkable form of Damodara and hearing His flute-song, Sri Jiva fainted. After a long time he finally regained consciousness, hut his heart still overflowed with love, and his eyes with tears.

Jiva Gosvami had another special Deity, one he received after the passing of Rupa Gosvami: the Govardhana-sila that Krsna had personally given to the ageing Sanatana Gosvami.

In New Vraja-dhama we also keep a Govardhana-sila with Radha-Damodara. Devotees call the sila “Little Giri." His surface shows some striking imprints of flowers and flower petals.

Devotees who cannot worship Radha-Damodara during the year must not neglect to do so during the month of Karttika, for this worship is compulsory for all sincere devotees of the Lord.

Scripture emphasises worship of Radha-Damodara: “Sri Radhika is Lord Hari’s favourite amongst the gopis, therefore during the month of Karttika She should he worshipped next to Sri Damodara.”

The same text then prescribes singing the Damodarastaka: “One should recite regularly the hymn sung by Satyavrata Rsi called Damodarastaka, because this worship attracts Sri Damodara.”

Srila Prabhupada also instructed that while chanting Damodarastaka we should offer a ghee lamp to the Lord, an offering equal in potency to performing all sorts of ablutions, austerities, and sacrifices.

Finally, devotees are urged to perform some extra austerity or vow for Karttika, such as bathing in cold water, eating only once a day, chanting extra rounds of japa, or reading a sacred text in its entirety.

By worshipping Lord Damodara for the duration of His month, devotees purify themselves of sinful reactions, mature in pure devotion, and qualify themselves for returning back to Godhead.

Such attainments are confirmed by the story of the sudrani named Kalipriya who lived in Treta-yuga. Dissatisfied with her husband, she was always hankering for a paramour.

Kalipriya loathed her pious husband. She spoke harshly to him, fed him all kinds of unclean foods, neglected her household duties, and tortured him by acting inappropriately in the presence of other men.

Fate finally satisfied Kalipriyā’s desire and sent her a paramour with whom she planned to elope. On the appointed night, while her lover waited for her in the jungle, Kalipriyā slit her husband’s throat.

Meanwhile, in the jungle, her lover was attacked and killed by a tiger. Finding her dead lover at the meeting place so shocked Kalipriyā that she regained her senses, and she lamented having killed her husband.

Wanting to atone for her sins, she went to another city where she saw Vaiṣṇavīs worshipping Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa with fruits, flowers, and incense while singing songs, reciting prayers, and blowing conches.

Kalipriyā asked the women, “Please tell me what you are doing.” They answered, “This is the beginning of Kārttika and we are worshipping Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa to remove all sin and elevate us to Their abode.”

Kalipriyā felt some hope. She accepted the ūrja-vrata and practised it strictly. At its completion, on the full moon day, freed of all sinful reaction, she gave up her body on the bank of the Narmadā River.

At that moment the messengers of Yamarāja arrived, but just as they were about to drag her soul away, Viṣṇu’s messengers also appeared. They interceded on Kalipriyā’s behalf and dismissed the Yamadūtas.

The Viṣṇudūtas placed Kalipriyā on their chariot and took her to the Lord’s abode to serve the divine couple directly. Such is the power of observing the worship of Rādhā and Dāmodara during Kārttika.

The sin of neglecting to observe this vow, however, nullifies one’s brahminical status, the benefit of ten years of pious life, and the good results of one’s hard work. In short, one falls down from spiritual life.

Devotees often ask why we worship Rādhā-Dāmodara during the month of Kārttika. Doesn’t the Dāmodarāṣṭaka prayer describe Mother Yaśodā catching and chastising her son for stealing butter?

The final verse answers this question, namo rādhikāyai tvadīya-priyāyai / namo ’nanta-līlāya devāya tubhyam: “I offer my obeisances unto Your beloved Rādhikā and unto to Your unlimited pastimes.”

Sanātana Gosvāmī’s commentary describes that this last line effectively offers the entire prayer in praise of Rādhā and Dāmodara and Their intimate pastimes, including the rāsa dance.

In conclusion, reciting the sweet childhood pastime of Dāmodara-Kṛṣṇa is the best way for non-devotees, devotees, and siddhas alike to worship Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa, and to become qualified to serve Them.

By praising Yaśodā-Dāmodara we please not only the divine couple but our own hearts as well. Gradually, then, we become inclined to the loving service of Kārttika-devī and Dāmodara.

Actually, the name Dāmodara in Rādhā-Dāmodara does not refer to the Kṛṣṇa whom Yaśodā bound by rope to a mortar, for at that age He wasn’t mixing with gopīs, not to mention playing with Śrī Rādhā.

Dāmodara here refers to another pastime, in which Rādhā, slightly angry with Kṛṣṇa, tied Him to the spot with a dismissive glance, just as Yaśodā had tied Him with rope to a grinding mortar.

The difference between these two pastimes is that when Rādhā tied Kṛṣṇa, He could not move at all. No one could set Him free until She undid the noose of Her glance, at which point He bowed at Her feet.

All glories to Rādhā and Dāmodara, the life of Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī, Jīva Gosvāmī, and Śrīla Prabhupāda. I worship the feet of these great souls for sharing their wealth with a fallen soul such as I.

On the Jaladuta, Śrīla Prabhupāda wrote, “Today the ship is plying very smoothly. I feel better. But I am feeling separation from Śrī Vṛndāvana and my Lords Govinda, Gopīnātha, Rādhā-Dāmodara.”

Remembering how Radha and Damodara agreed to accept my service and to stay in Krsna Valley, I live here happily, always embracing within my heart the following anthem of the residents of this land.

*Giri-vara-tata-kutire manju-vrndavanesa-sarasi cd racayan sri-radhika-syama-kirtim*  
*dhrta-rati ramaniyam samsmarams tat-padabjam nava-vraja-phalam asnan sarva-kalam vasami*

**“May I eternally live in a cottage between Radha’s lake and the best of hills, chant Radha-Syama’s names as I think of Them with love, and eat the water, grains, and fruits of New Vraja-dhama.”**

At the eastern end of Radha-Damodara’s garden is a small forest that marks the place where the Gosvamis would meet, discuss how to spread Krsna consciousness, and honour prasada.

Although the Gosvamis had their own residences and temples in different parts of Vrndavana, they would often meet there under the shelter of Seva-kunja, Radha-Damodara, and Rupa Gosvami.

Just a little south, in the area next to a beautiful weeping willow that stands by the shore of Balabhadra-kunda, is Bada-bathain, marking the place where young boys and girls come on the day after Holi.

The girls would carry bamboo sticks, hoping to strike the boys in reprisal for any teasing the girls may have suffered during that year. Sometimes the boys carried shields for protection; otherwise they ran.

At other times of the year, Nanda Maharaja would gather the senior cowherd men of Vrndavana at this place to resolve interpersonal conflicts and devise community strategies.

During the time of the Gosvamis, the Vraja-vasis continued to meet in that place. They even requested Sanatana Gosvami to take up residence nearby and act as their advisor at such ista-gosthis.

Remembering the pastimes of Bada-bathain benefits the leaders of New Vraja-dhama when they hold their own meetings, counseling or mediation sessions, or even judicial hearings.

The garden’s landscaping between Bada-bathain and Nikunja-mandira — the back end of Radha-Damodara’s temple — resembles the enchanting scriptural descriptions of the sub-forests of Vraja.

With its flowers, flowering bushes, shade-giving trees, and fruit orchards, Damodaravana attracts sundry birds, bees, and even devotees looking for a secluded place to perform nama-sankirtana.

Within Damodaravana is a rose garden (Gula-camana) that features a grove of climbing roses and a small lake called Gula-kunda, all created for the pleasure of Radha-Damodara and Radha-Syamasundara.

I worship the Supreme Person, whose waist is bound by the rope of loving devotion as He wanders like a moving blue land-lotus within a charming forest, following the nectarean footprints of sulking Radha.

**\* \* \* \***

West of Damodaravana is the forest called Nipavana, where secluded Krsna-kunda provides a place for water play for Radha and Krsna in summer, and where the samadhi of Srila Jiva Gosvami now stands.

After leaving Navadvipa, Sri Jiva followed the instruction of Lord Nityananda and travelled to Varanasi, where he studied under Madhusudana Sarasvati, a disciple of Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya.

His teacher professed the doctrine of monism, but Jiva took advantage of his expertise in Sanskrit, grammar, Vedanta, logic, and in branches of learning like purva-mimamsa and karma-mimamsa.

His studies completed, Jiva Gosvami then joined his uncles Rupa and Sanatana, who tutored Jiva in Srimad-Bhagavatam, as well as the Vaisnava scriptures being written by the Gosvamis at that rime.

Between his arrival in Vrndavana in 1535 and Rupa Gosvamis disappearance in 1564, Jiva Gosvami worshipped Radha and Damodara, edited Rupa Gosvami’s books, and wrote books of his own.

On the sad day of Rupa Gosvamis disappearance in the month of Sravana, senior Vaisnavas like Raghunatha Dasa and Gopala Bhatta Gosvamis selected Sri Jiva as the acarya of all Gaudiya Vaisnavas.

Jiva Gosvami was universally accepted as the Gaudiyas’ foremost scholar, and so the Radha-Damodara temple compound became a school where devotees went to learn philosophy and theology.

Like Jiva Gosvami, Srila Prabhupada presented the essence of the Vedas in contemporary language and context, establishing ISKCON as the educational institution that would assimilate these ideas.

His Divine Grace emphasised again and again that his followers, in order to remain fixed in Krsna consciousness, must study his books as well as those of our previous acaryas, especially those of Jiva Gosvami.

Srila Prabhupada writes,

**“To understand Lord Caitanya, one has to follow the direct disciples of Lord Caitanya — the Six Gosvamis — and especially the path chalked out by Srila Jiva Gosvami.”**

Sri Jiva called his school the Visva-vaisnava Raja-sabha. That would later be the name of Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura’s institution before it became known as the Gaudiya Matha.

Vaisnavas from Orissa and Bengal flocked to Vrndavana, most notably Narottama, Syamananda, and Srinivasa, who were to be emissaries in eastern India of the Gosvamis’ teachings.

The acarya welcomed everyone, as Srila Prabhupada writes,

**“Jiva Gosvami was very kind to the Gaudiya Vaisnavas. Whoever went to Vrndavana he provided with a residence and prasada.”**

Jiva Gosvami’s spiritual stature, vast erudition, and well-reputed institution attracted scholars from all over India. Radha-Damodara temple thus became the headquarters of the Gaudiya Vaisnavas.

In 1966 Srila Prabhupada thought to revive the Radha-Damodara temple’s lost academic tradition by constructing a Rupanuga-para-vidya-pitha to act as a base for his western students in Vrndavana.

Unfortunately, the temple management rejected Srila Prabhupada’s proposal. Today, ISKCON is connected with the Radha-Damodara temple mainly through the rooms Srila Prabhupada occupied there.

In addition to his other responsibilities, Jiva Gosvami, like Srila Prabhupada, wrote many books; indeed, his literary output is astounding, exceeding in verses the Puranic compositions of Vyasa.

Sri Java's primary literary contributions — the Sat-sandarbhas — provide a written record of Lord Caitanya’s teachings and, like Srila Prabhupada’s books, defeat the impersonal school of thought.

Baladeva Vidyabhusana praises Jiva Gosvami,

*yah sankhya-pankena kutarka-pamsuna vivarta-gartena ca lupta-didhitim*  
*suddham vyadhad vak-sudhaya krsnam sa jivah prabhur astu no gatih*

**“By the mighty spade of his nectarean words Jiva Gosvami tossed aside the dirt of fallacious Sankhya arguments and recovered Krsna’s splendour from the pit of Vivartavada. May he be our refuge!"**

Jiva Gosvami lived in Vrndavana for sixty-five years. In 1596, during the month of Pausa, he went back to Godhead to fully engage in the service of Radha and Damodara as the gopi Vilasa-manjari.

According to his will, Jiva Gosvami’s sacred body was interred close to Radha and Damodara, and his properties and library were transferred to his successor, Sri Vilasa Dasa, who became the temple’s custodian.

Let us hear a few lines of a song composed by Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, sung to commemorate the disappearance of Srila Jiva Gosvami. We can also recite these verses while visiting Java's samadhi.

*Mu ati mudamati, toma vinu nahi gati, sri Jiva jivana prana-dhana*

**“O Sri Jiva Prabhu! This foolish rascal cannot survive without your mercy, which sustains his verylife.”**

*Bahu janma punya kari, durlabha janama dhari, paiyachi sri jiva carana*

**“After many births of pious acts I have taken this rare birth, in which I’ve attained the service of the lotus feet of Sri Jiva.”**

*Sri jiva karuna-sindhu, sparsi tara eka bindu, prema-ratna pabara lagiya*

**“By touching a drop from Sri Java's ocean of mercy I have attained the jewel of prema, pure love for Radha and Krsna.”**

*Kabe raghunatha dasa, tuya anugata asa, rakha more pada chaya diya*

**“Raghunatha Dasa says, ‘O Sri Jiva! My only desire is to he your follower. Please protect me and give me the shade of your lotus feet.’”**

The next place of pilgrimage is the samadhi of the great poet Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami. In Bhauma Vrndavana, some say his tomb is at Radha-kunda, others, at the Radha-Damodara temple.

Lack of historical records related to the lives of acaryas like Rupa Gosvami or Jiva Gosvami obscures even basic information, such as their dates of birth and death and other chronological details of their lives.

But then the notion of history in both the Vaisnava and Vedic traditions focuses less on the linear flow from one event in time to another and more on defining moments and achievements.

Kaviraja Gosvami is another example of a great Vaisnava about whom little is known. What we do know is limited to the events that he and his associates considered important enough to record.

He came to Vrndavana at a young age and lived the rest of his life at Radha-kunda writing the two masterpieces for which he has been immortalised.

Jiva Gosvami honoured him with the title “Kaviraja.”

Two features of Kaviraja Gosvami’s life stand out: his taking up the order to write Sri Caitanya-caritamrta very late in life, and his unmatched ability to write beautiful, rhythmic, poetic verse.

From his own writing we know that Kaviraja Gosvami completed his magnum opus a short time before he passed away — again, date unknown. He was also completely indifferent to his own comfort.

Just as our beloved Srila Prabhupada left Vrndavana at an advanced age to fulfill his guru’s order, Kaviraja Gosvami, in failing health, accepted the order of the Vaisnavas to record Sri Caitanya’s pastimes.

Having recorded the basic truths of Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s identity and His teachings, and then having prefaced the Lord’s pastimes, Kaviraja Gosvami marvelled at the colossal task ahead of him.

He writes, “I have now become too old and disturbed by invalidity. While writing, my hands tremble. I cannot remember anything, nor can I see or hear properly. Still I write, and this is a great wonder.”

In 1615, a date recorded in the text, he completed the Caitanya-caritamrta by the grace of Rupa Gosvami and Raghunatha Gosvami, to whom he dedicated every chapter. That was an even greater wonder.

Kaviraja Gosvami’s poetic style is distinctively sweet and uncluttered. Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura used to say that people the world over would learn Bengali just to relish the poetry in its original form.

This is but a taste,

*kauravinda-kanti-nindi citra-patta-satika krsna-matta-bhrngra keli-phulla-puspa-vatika*  
*krsna-nitya-sangamartha padmabandhu-radhika mahyam atma-pada-padma-dasyadastu radhika*

**“May Radhika, whose wonderful and exquisite garments rebuke the splendour of rubies, who is a garden of blossoming flowers where the maddened bumblebee Krsna plays, and who worships the sun god —**

**“To attain Krsna’s eternal companionship, give me service to Her lotus feet.” This verse is from a chapter in Govinda-lilamrta entitled “The Parrots Eulogise Radha and Krsna.”**

Feeling greatly indebted for his unparalleled literary contribution, and for his exemplary Vaisnava character, let us offer our respects to Kaviraja Gosvami by quoting the words of Narottama Dasa Thakura:

**“Amongst the devotees, Krsnadasa Kaviraja is extremely expert at relishing the mellows of love of God. When the stones hear his prose in praise of Lord Caitanya and Sri Govinda, they melt inecstasy.”**

Surrounding Kaviraja Gosvami’s samadhi is the garden Bedokhora-kunja, rich with the divine couple’s pastimes. The first weeping willow planted in New Vraja-dhama marks its inner chamber.

Radha and Krsna keep the doors of this secluded grove tightly closed. Its walls are difficult to see through. Thus the pastimes They perform there are known only to Them and Their most intimate devotees.

In the eastern corner of this grove is Chota-baithana. Krsna and Balarama rested here while tending Their cows. Then, after regaining Their strength, They played games as Their herd grazed peacefully.

As usual, the gopas were so engrossed in play that their limbs, clothes, and hair were all coated with dust. They looked white, like ghosts, and so they had a hearty laugh at one another’s expense.

But Balarama knew that Mother Yasoda would he angry with Him if He brought Krsna home in that state, so He guided the boys to nearby Kuntala-kunda, where He, Krsna, and the other boys bathed.

After drying themselves off, the gopas decorated Krsna’s hair with feathers and flowers they collected in the grove. And when they were presentable once again, they started their journey home.

On another occasion, Radha, attracted by the cooling shade and peaceful atmosphere of this grove, stopped here with Her friends. After walking through the garden, She sat beside the lake.

Absorbed in thoughts of the loving exchanges Radha and Krsna had shared in the confines of this grove, Her maidservants quickly collected flowers and decorated Her hair.

Since the waters of this lake, as well as the surrounding flower garden, rendered service to the hair (kuntala) of both Radha and Krsna, the lake came to be known as Kuntala-kunda.

**\* \* \* \***

North of Bedokhora-kunja, on the opposite side of the Parikrama path, is Syamananda Pandita’s Tilaka-sthana and, next to that, his puspa-samadhi, which in New Vraja-dhama is the ladies’ asrama.

In Bhauma Vrndavana these two places are close together, near the temples of Radha-Damodara and Radha-Syamasundara. The latter Deities were established and worshipped by Syamananda Prabhu.

Syamananda Prabhu was known as Duhkhi Krsna Dasa when he first came to the Radha-Damodara temple to study under Jiva Gosvami. Duhkhi Krsna’s daily service was to sweep Seva-kunja.

The practice of serving the dhama is just as old as circumamhulating it. Pilgrims and residents alike know well that without serving holy places no one can fully reap the spiritual rewards those places hestow.

Just as Duhkhi Krsna eagerly swept away the thorns and fallen leaves in Seva-kunja, the pilgrims — and especially the residents — of New Vraja-dhama should eagerly serve its holy places.

Such dhama-seva can take the form of weeding gardens, cleaning ponds, sweeping the ground, securing trees to their supports, planting and caring for flowers, or cleaning paving stones and pavilions.

As the scholar Duhkhi Krsna swept Seva-kunja, he thought, “This is the same dust that once touched the feet of Krsna and His gopis.” And as he did so he muddied the earth with a steady stream of tears.

Duhkhi Krsna’s humble, faithful attitude to the dust of the dhama so endeared him to the queen of Vrndavana, Srimati Radharani, that She decided to bestow Her causeless mercy uponhim.

While sweeping a corner of Seva-kunja, Duhkhi Krsna Dasa came upon a brilliant anklet whose golden bejewelled handiwork, texture, and even fragrance appeared otherworldly.

Duhkhi Krsna held the ornament in his hand, and currents of divine ecstasy surged through his body and mind. Such feelings could only come by the touch of a gopi, perhaps only the touch of Radha Herself.

Thinking that the owner of the anklet would soon return to reclaim it, Duhkhi Krsna tied it into a corner of his cloth and tried his best to continue sweeping, despite the disturbances of his newfound ecstasy.

Meanwhile, Radha noticed that one of Her anklets had fallen off. She at once requested Lalita to find it. Generally, such a service would go to the maidservants, but Radha had Her reasons for sending Lalita.

Without hesitating, Lalita-devi headed for Seva-kunja, disguised as an ordinary village girl. There she approached Duhkhi Krsna Dasa and asked him if he had found an anklet that her sister had lost.

Duhkhi Krsna’s spiritual intuition told him that this village girl was anything hut ordinary. He concluded that she was hiding her true identity and therefore refused to give her the anklet.

“I think I found what you are looking for,” he said, “but how could simple villagers afford such a costly item?” “We may be villagers, but we are not poor.” Lalita curtly replied, “Please give me the anklet.”

Duhkhi Krsna persisted, “Dear girl! I want to see for myself if your sister is as wealthy as you insist she is. Please take me to her. If what you say is true, I will happily hand over the anklet.”

Lalita-devi’s temper was heating up, but she could recognise Duhkhi Krsna’s spiritual competence and so restrained herself. She took the young man to nearby Lalita-kunda and told him to bathe.

Duhkhi Krsna was perplexed. Was this girl’s sister so aristocratic that he had to bathe before meeting her? He asked, “Please tell me why I must bathe?” Lalita, arms akimbo, tapped her feet impatiently.

Things were progressing more slowly than she would have liked, so Lalita-devi assumed her original form. Beholding the unrivalled beauty of Radha’s foremost friend, Duhkhi Krsna Dasa fainted.

Lalita revived Duhkhi Krsna with the touch of her hand. When she again asked him to bathe in the lake, he gladly entered Lalita-kunda, fully dressed, and completely submerged himself.

Upon surfacing, he found that he had taken on the form of a gopi dressed and ornamented as a Vraja-vasi. Duhkhi Krsna was beside himself in bliss, but by Lalita s influence he was able to act normally.

The two girls then entered the unmanifest, eternal feature of Vrndavana. Lalita escorted this new gopi to a grove in which Srimati Radharani sat on a throne surrounded by Her friends.

The ecstasy of seeing his worshipable Goddess was too much for Duhkhi Krsna. To the amusement and curiosity of the other gopis, he again fainted, this time at the foot of Sri Radha’s throne.

And, once again, Lalita-devi roused Syamananda. “Here is my sister,” Lalita said to him. “You wanted to know whether She could have been the rightful owner of that anklet. So now what do you think?”

In his gopi body, Duhkhi Krsna Dasa gazed at Sri Radha, the gopis, and the kunja, his senses and mind overwhelmed by the inconceivable grandeur of the sweet rural atmosphere surrounding him.

Unable to speak, he simply reached out to give Radha the magical anklet, and Sri Radha pulled Duhkhi Krsna towards Her with Her right hand as She gratefully accepted the ornament with Her left.

Nodding Her head in approval, Radha said, “I am very pleased with your devotion and with the service you render by sweeping. I want to bless you through the anklet you have brought back to Me.”

Sri Radha then pressed Her anklet against Duhki Krsna’s forehead, leaving a mark that resembled a U. And if that blessing wasn’t enough, Radha personally gave him a Deity of Krsna and a new name.

Smiling, Radha said, “From today your name will be Syamananda. Continue in your service and one day you will attain Me forever.” Syamananda bowed down, but when he looked up all had changed.

Once again he was in the garden where he had found the anklet. And once again he was in the clothes and body of a brahmacari. Radha, Lalita, and the eternal realm of Vrndavana had vanished.

But the Deity of Krsna in his hands and the mark on his forehead reassured Syamananda that he had not been dreaming. In trance, he returned to Jiva Gosvami and told the acarya everything.

Why did Radha send Lalita to bring Syamananda to Her? Because without the grace of Lalita-devi, no one can come in direct contact with Sri Radha, even if they have the sponsorship of some other gopi.

Dear devotees! Let us glean from this pastime how important sincere service to Vrndavana-dhama is to Srimati Radharani. It is, after all, as dear to Her as Krsna, because the dhama is no different from Him.

Let us humbly serve Radha’s and Syamasundara playground with pure devotion. Then one day, like Syamananda, the queen of Vrndavana Herself may reward us with gifts from Her own hand.

If Radha and Syama happily cultivate the trees and creepers of Vraja, play with its birds and creatures, bathe in its lakes, sport in its forest, and praise it with Their own tongues, why shouldn’t we?

Sri Radha had ordered Syamananda Prabhu to preach in Orissa, and so he left Vraja with Srinivasa and Narottama and, with the help of his disciple Rasikananda, spread the glories of nama-sankirtana.

While touring Orissa, Syamananda Pandita engaged everyone he met in nama-sankirtana. By his divine influence the whole country appeared to be turning to Vaisnavism.

But in one town Sher Khan, a devout Muslim ruler, determined to protect his subjects from being misled, went with his soldiers to confront Syamananda and his sankirtana procession.

“If you continue this chanting,” Sher Khan threatened, “I will have you all flogged and chained.” Syamananda relented and went home with his devotees, but the next day they all returned to the streets.

The devotees were apprehensive, but Syamananda was oblivious to everything except Lord Caitanya’s order and the bliss of chanting Krsna’s holy names. Sher Khan soon heard of Syamananda’s boldness.

Determined to take decisive action against the Vaisnava upstarts, Sher Khan and a contingent of armed soldiers marched to the marketplace where Syamananda’s massive sankirtana was underway.

Sher Khan was not one tor empty threats: his soldiers smashed the devotees’ drums and tore the cymbals from their hands. But before they could do more Syamananda called out to Radha and Krsna.

Syamananda’s chanting was like Prahlada Maharaja’s chanting as he was tortured by his father, or Haridasa Thakura’s chanting as he was whipped in the marketplaces. It was fully transcendental.

Suddenly the hair and the beards of the king and his soldiers caught tire. The flames quickly spread to their clothes and forced the entire army to seek shelter by jumping, weapons and all, into a nearby lake.

Humiliated before his subjects, Sher Khan and his men retreated to the palace, where the king sequestered himself in his quarters, unable to comprehend what had happened and why.

That night Allah came to Sher Khan in a dream and angrily slapped him. Allah told the king that His name and the name of Krsna were the same, and that Lord Caitanya was His most confidential form.
