Tapana Misra awoke, but had no idea where to find this Nimai Pandita. The Lord, however, playing the pastime of a dig-vijaya Pandita, had already arrived at Misra’s village, along with Lokanatha.

Tapana Misra prostrated himself before the Lord and asked, “O Prabhu! I cannot find peace in this world. Please tell me about the ultimate goal of life and how to achieve it.”

Nimai Pandita spoke the following famous words: “O brahmana! In this Age of Kali the only sadhana by which you can cross the ocean of material existence is nama-sankirtana. There is no other way.

“Day and night, chant this mantra of thirty-two letters and sixteen names of Sri Krsna without offence:

*Hare Krsna, Hare Krsna, Krsna Krsna, Hare Hare*  
*Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare.*

“If you continually chant this maha-mantra, the seed of love of God will sprout in your heart, at which time you will completely understand the goal of life and the process for achieving it.”

Tapana Misra wanted to accompany Caitanya Mahaprabhu wherever the Lord travelled, but Sri Caitanya ordered him to move to Varanasi, assuring the brahmana that they would meet there in due course.

Just before Caitanya Mahaprabhu departed for Navadvipa He embraced Tapana Misra and whispered, “Whatever you heard about Me in your dream is true. But do not disclose it to anyone.”

Tapana Misra moved to Varanasi in 1503. In 1515 Lord Caitanya stopped at Varanasi on His way to Vraja. While bathing at Manikarnika-ghata, He was finally reunited with Tapana Misra.

At Tapana Misra’s request, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu remained in Varanasi for ten days and instructed His devotee in the details of Krsna consciousness and how to achieve perfection.

By that time Tapana Misra’s son, Raghunatha Bhatta, was ten years old. Just as the young Gopala Bhatta had served the Lord with love in Sri Ranga, Raghunatha now served the Lord in Varanasi.

On His return to Varanasi from Vrndavana, Caitanya Mahaprabhu met Sanatana Gosvami. For two full months, while He instructed Sanatana, the Lord stayed at the house of Candrasekhara Acarya.

During that visit to Varanasi, the Lord would regularly accept His meals at the home of Tapana Misra. When Lord Caitanya left for Jagannatha Puri, He again instructed His followers to remain there.

Varanasi was the capital of Mayavada philosophy, where only the mantras so ’ham and tat tvam asi could he heard. But all that changed once Lord Caitanya had converted Prakasananda Sarasvati.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu had taught Tapana Misra and Candrasekhara the principles of Krsna consciousness. Now He wanted His devotees to give their companionship to the newly converted Mayawadis.

For the rest of their lives the two brahmanas followed the Lord’s order, embracing the principle that vaniseva is superior to vapuh-seva, and the Lord greatly appreciated their conviction.

When Raghunatha Bhatta met Lord Caitanya in Puri and informed Him that his parents had passed away, the Lord spoke to His associates at length about Tapana Misra’s deep devotion to Sri Krsna.

Did Tapana Misra attain the perfection he had so diligently sought throughout his life? Yes, according to Lord Narayana’s words to Aditi: “Yet because I have been satisfied hy the activities of your vow —

“O goddess Aditi, I must find some means to favour you, for worship of Me, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, never goes in vain but certainly gives the desired result according to what one deserves.”

Next to these three samadhis is Sanatana Gosvamis bhajana-kutira, where he first awaited Lord Caitanya’s arrival in Vrndavana and later served Madana-gopala, as we will shortly hear.

In New Vraja-dhama, the bhajana-kutira is situated on a southward sloping hill called Dvadasaditya-tila (“the hill of the twelve suns”), which is linked to Krsna's pastime of chastising the Kaliya serpent.

Many hours passed between the moment Krsna first jumped into the Yamuna and the time He finally banished Kaliya from Vrndavana. Now He was soaking wet and shivering from cold.

His mother dried Him off and wrapped Him in a blanket, but Krsna was still shivering. He climbed up Dvadasaditya-tila to escape the cool shade of the forest and to warm Himself in the afternoon sun.

But due to the late hour, the sun was weak. The twelve sun gods then personally surrounded Krsna and warmed Him with their rays until His chill was gone and He was perspiring profusely.

Indeed, Krsna’s perspiration flowed down the hill and formed a small lake that became known as Praskandana-kunda. A rose garden by the bank of the Yamuna also shares the name of this holy place.

Scriptures say that one who bathes or worships the sun god there will happily enter the sun planet. If one performs these rituals on a Sunday, one can also enjoy the fulfilment of all desires in this life.

Elsewhere it is said that merely by seeing Dvadasaditya-tila one becomes free of sins. But the best result comes from remembering Krsna’s pastimes: one becomes devoted to the Lord.

Not far from Praskandana-kunda is Ganga-kunda, where for the sake of His companions Krsna invoked the Ganges. A bath there is equivalent to one hundred baths in the Ganges River itself.

During Caitanya Mahaprabhu’s visit to Vrndavana, He came to Dvadasaditya-tila and very much appreciated the secluded, sacred atmosphere of the place. He vowed to return someday.

Indeed, Lord Caitanya told His disciples that He was planning to return to Vrndavana, and He specifically told Sanatana Gosvami that He would come to live with him on Dvadasaditya-tila.

But Lord Caitanya did not return. Sanatana Gosvami waited and waited, until he thought he would likely die of separation. At that time the Lord thought it wise to appear to His devotee in a dream.

Sanatana saw Lord Caitanya seated on a throne that exuded such beauty, influence, and effulgence that Sanatana felt compelled to fall prostrate before the Lord and grasp hold of His lotus feet.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu lifted Sanatana Gosvami to his feet, consoled him, and then disappeared; thus, even though the Lord did not come physically to Dvadasaditya, He fulfilled His promise in another way.

Let us always remember Sanatana Gosvami and never forget him, for by so doing our lives are auspiciously intertwined with he whom Kavi-karnapura identified as an extension of Lord Caitanya’s body.

*Ye rupa-manjari-prestha purasit rati-manjari socyate nama-bhedena lavanga-manjari budhaih*  
*sadya gaura bhinna tanuh sarvaradhyah*  
*Sanatanah tam eva pravisat karyat-muni-ratnam sanatanah*

**“Rupa-manjari’s closest friend, known by the names Rati-mahjari and Lavariga-manjari, appeared as Sanatana Gosvami, He was honoured by everyone and respected as an extension of Lord Caitanya’s body.**

**“The son of Brahma, Sanatana Kumara, the jewel amongst the sages, entered the body of Sanatana Gosvami, who is therefore the combined incarnation of sage and gopi.”**

**\* \* \* \***

Just next to Sanatana Gosvami’s bhajana-kutira in New Vraja- dhama, within a charming cottage nestled amongst thickset trees atop glorious Dvadasaditya-tila, is the temple of Radha-Madana-mohana.

Revered by Gaudiya Vaisnavas as the Deities who bestow knowledge of the self and of the Lord, Radha-Madana-mohana initially awaken us to our relationship (sambandha) with Krsna.

The residents of New Vraja-dhama honour and serve pictures of various Deities, such as Radha-Madana-mohana, as different features of Radha-Syamasundara, their most worshipful Lords.

Just as all Deities in Bhauma Vrndavana are manifestations of Radha and Krsna, pictures of those same Deities revered here in New Vraja-dhama arc seen as manifestations of Radha and Syama.

At Dvadasaditya-tila, Radha-Syamasundara, in the form of Radha-Madana-mohana, kindly help us to establish our relationship with Them. How could we approach or serve Them if They did not?

In gratitude we offer this prayer: “Glory to the all-merciful Radha and Madana-mohana! I am lame and ill advised, yet They are my directors, and Their lotus feet are everything to me.”

Space does not permit me to glorify all the Deities revered in New Vraja-dhama. But Madana-mohana is both the root of and the water upon the creeper of our devotion, thus I offer special praise to Him.

Hearing the pastimes and qualities of Madana-mohana can give us a drop of insight into how much the Lord is willing to go out of His way to reclaim those fallen souls who have willfully abandoned Him.

O learned Vaisnavas! I begin my description of Madana-mohana, who is also known as Madana-gopala, with a beautiful prayer by Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, which I present in full for your pleasure.

Please enjoy reading this beautifully poetic prayer. If ii attracts your heart, recite it regularly. You will see from the concluding blessing (phala-stuti) that the benefit of doing so is simply wonderful.

Dasa Gosvami writes,

*vana-bhuvi ravikanya-svaccha-kacchali-pali dhvani-yutad-vara tirtha-dvadasaditya-kunje*  
*sa-kanaka-mani-vedi madhya-madhyadhirudhah sphurati madana-purvah ?? ,pi gopala esah*

**“On the shore of the Yamuna in the Dvadasaditya forest, filled with humming bees in the middle of a courtyard of gold and jewels, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Subhaga-nava-sikhanda-bhrajad usnisa-harangada-valaya-samudra dhvana-manjira-ramyah*  
*vasana-ghusrna-carca-mallikollasitangah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“Wearing a splendid new peacock-feather crown, necklaces, armlets, bracelets, rings, and anklets, His limbs covered in fine garments, saffron, and jasmine flowers, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Kali-krta-vara-bhanga nyasta-janghanya-janghah kara-yuga-dhrta-vamsim nyasta-bimbadharagre*  
*su-madhuram ati-tiryag-grivaya vadayams tdm sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“Sweetly playing the flute He holds with both hands to His bimba-fruit lips, His waist and knees bent, His neck tilted, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Vidhi-krta-vidhu-srsti vyarthata-kari-vaktra-dyuti lava-hrta-radha-sthula-manandhakarah*  
*smita-lapita-madhulyonmaditaitad dhrsikah sphurati madana-purvah ko ’pi gopala esah*

**“The sweetness of—**  
**“His smiles and words maddening Radha’s senses, a ray of the splendour of His face removing the blinding darkness of Her anger, proving Brahma’s moon to be useless, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Sarad-udita-saroja-vrata-vilrasi netran-cala-kutila-kataksair mand-aroddanda-calaih*  
*jhatiti mathita-radha-svanta-dugdharnavantah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“His crooked sidelong glances frightening the autumnal lotus flowers and churning the depths of the milk ocean of Radha’s heart with the rod of Mount Mandara, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Kutila-catula-cilli-valli-lasyena labdha-prathita-sakala sadhvi-dharma-ratna-prasadah*  
*tilakavad-alikena dhvasta-kamesu-capah sphurati madana-purvah ?? ’pi gopala esah*

**“The dancing of the crooked vines of His eyebrows gaining for Him the kindness of jewel-like saintly girls, and His tilaka-graced forehead destroying the bow of Kama, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Suka-yuva-vara-cancu pramsu-nasamsu-sindhau janita-kula-vadhuti-drsti-matsyi-viharah*  
*smita-lava-yuta-radha-alpa-mantronmadantah sphurati madana-purvah ko’pi gopala esah*

**“Hypnotised by the mantra of Radha’s smiling words, His nose more handsome than a parrot’s beak, this ocean of beauty, in which the fish of chaste girls’ glances play, Madana-gopala is sublimely manifest.”**

*Vikasad-adhara-bandhukantar uddiya gandhaih patitam upa vidhartum radhikd-citta-bhrngam*  
*dasana-ruci-gunagre datta-tat-sidhu-carah sphu-rati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“Baiting with nectar the noose of His teeth’s splendour, capturing the bee of Radha’s heart as it lands on the flower of His lips to smell its sweetness, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest."**

*Sravana-madana-kanda-preksanoddina radha-dhrti-vibhava-vihange nyasta-netranta-banah*  
*alaka-madhupa-datta dyota-madhvika-sattrah sphurati madana-purvah ?? ’pi gopala esah*

**“Offering the honey of beauty to His forehead’s bumblebee locks, aiming the arrow of His glance at the bird of Radha’s composure as it flies to see the charm of His ears, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Parimala-ruci-pali-sali gandharvikodyan-mukha kamala-madhudi-pana-matta-dvirephah*  
*mukura-jayi-kapole mrgya-tac-cumba-bimbah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi go paid esah*

**“Assuming the role of a black bee drunk on the liquor of Radha’s fragrant lotus face, searching Her lustrous cheeks for the bimba fruits of Her lips, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Makara-mukha-sadrksa-svarna varnavatamsa-pracalana-hrta-radha sarva-sarira-dharmah*  
*tad-ati-cala-drg-anta-svastha-vamse dhrtaksah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“The movements of His golden, shark-shaped earrings making Radha forget all material duties, His eyes meeting Her restless sidelong glance at His flute, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Harimani-krta-sankha slaghitollanghi-lekha traya-ruci-vrta-kanthasya upakanthe manindram*  
*dadhad iha parirabdhum radhikam bimbitam ca sphurati madana-purvah ko ‘pi gopala esah*

**“Placing on His three-lined neck, already more glorious than a sapphire conch, the king of jewels that will lovingly reflect Radha’s image, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Kuvalaya-krta-vaksas-talpam uccam dadhanah srama-vilulita-radha-svapanayaiva navyam*  
*bhuja-yugam api divyam tat-prakandopadhanam sphurati madana-purvah k? 'pi gopala esah*

**“His lotus chest a great bed for exhausted Radharani to sleep on, His transcendental arms Her excellent pillow, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Rucira-jathara-patre citra-nabhi-tatodyat tanuruha-tati-namnim ballavi-vrnda-bhuktyai*  
*smara-nrpaii-samudra-svaksaralim dadhanah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“His abdomen a charming leaf King Kamadeva inscribed with his own names for the pleasure of the gopis, with the wonderful line of hairs rising from the navel, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Yuvati-hrd-alasebha praudha-bandhaya kama-sthapita-cita-rasoru stambha-jrmbhabhiramah*  
*marakata-kata-jaitra plulla-janu-prasannah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah:*

**“His glorious knees eclipsing a host of sapphires, His thighs the nectar-posts established by Kamadeva to tightly bind the elephants of the young gopis’ hearts, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Pranaya-nava-madhunam pana-matraika-gatya sakala-karana-jivyam radhika-matta-bhrngyah*  
*aruna-carana-kanja-dvandvam ullasya pasyan sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“His reddish lotus feet the life-force for the senses of the intoxicated bumblebee Radha, whose only wish is to drink the new honey of love there, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Atula-vilasad-anga-sreni-vinyasa-bhangya glapita-madana-koti sphara-saundarya-kirtih*  
*bala-lava-hata-mattapara-parindra-darpah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“The graceful movements of His limbs making the glory of a million Cupids wilt, and a speck of His strength humbling the arrogance of the greatest of lions, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Tarani-duhitr-kacche svaccha-pathodadhama samudita-nava-kamabhira-ramavalinam*  
*tadid-ati-ruci-bahu-sphujad-amso ‘ti-jrmbhan sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“Like a splendid monsoon cloud, with shoulders and arms embraced hy the lightning flashes of many amorous gopis as it moves along the Yamuna’s shore, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Nava-tarunima-bhattacarya-varyena sastram manasija-muni klptam nyayam adhyapitabhih*  
*nava-nava-yuvatibhir bibhrad-udgraham asmin sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“Debating with the young gopis, who studied the scriptures of Kamadeva Muni under the care of youth, their teacher, Sri Madana- gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Ratim ati-racayantya radhika-narma-kantya sthagita-vacana-darpah spharitanya-prasangah*  
*kharam ati-lalitasye kincid ancat-smitaksah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“His arrogant demands eclipsed by the splendour of Radha’s playfully delightful words, His crooked, smiling glance aimed at Lalita’s sharply intelligent face, Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Sa-vidha-ramita-radhah sagraja-snigdha-rupa pranaya-rucira-candrah kunja-khela-vitandrah*  
*racita-jana-cakora-prema-piyusa-varsah sphurati madana-purvah k? ’pi gopala esah*

**“A moon of love for His elder brother, never tiring of pastimes in the forest, happy to stay near Radha, showering the nectar of love on the Vraja-vasis, Sri Madana-gopala is splendidly manifest.”**

*Madana-valita-gopalasya yah stotram etat pathati sumatir udyad-dainya- vanyabhisiktah*  
*sa khalu visaya-ragam sauri-bhagam vihaya pratijani labhate tat-pada-kanjanuragam*

**“An intelligent person who has bathed in the waters of humility, abandoned all sense gratification leading to hell, and who reads this prayer to Madana-gopala, attains pure love for His lotus feet.”**

**\* \* \* \***

Some authorities suggest that Madana-gopala was the Deity worshipped by Ambarisa Maharaja during his year-long vow in Madhuvana. Sri Suka describes the king’s Deity worship there:

**“Following the regulative principles of mahabhiseka, Ambarisa performed the bathing ceremony for the Deity of Krsna with all ingredients, and then he dressed the Deity with clothes, ornaments —**

**“Fragrant flower garlands, and other paraphernalia for worship of the Lord. With attention and devotion, he worshipped Krsna and all the greatly fortunate brahmanas, who were free from material desires."**

Srimad-Bhagavatam does not mention Madana-gopala by name, but oral tradition confirms that when Vajranabha was developing Vraja, Madana-gopala was one of the eight Deities he excavated.

It is commonly accepted, however, that before Vajra discovered Madana-gopala, He was also the worshipable Deity of Kubja, whose Mathura home Krsna personally visited with Uddhava.

According to Srimad-Bhagavatam, Kubja asked Krsna to stay with her for a few days. Krsna promised to fulfill her desire, hut at that time He offered her respects and then left with Uddhava.

How did Krsna fulfill His promise to Kubja? Surely He visited her at a later time, hut devotees generally accept that His presence in her home as the Deity Madana-gopala was a way of keeping that promise.

In the millennia following Vajra’s revival of Vrndavana, the dhama, along with its temples and Deities, receded from view. During that time, Madana-gopala lay buried under the earth of Dvadasaditya-tila.

When Advaita Acarya visited Vrndavana, He would sit beneath a banyan tree a short distance downstream from the hill. One day, while on Parikrama, He discovered where Madana-gopala was buried.

Advaita Acarya felt blessed to have ihe service of Madana-gopala. He worshipped the Lord at what is now called Advaita-vata, until time obliged Advaita to return to Santipura and Navadvipa.

Before Advaita Acarya left for Bengal, concerned for the Deity’s safety, He took Madana-gopala to Mathura and placed Him in the care of a brahmana by the name of Damodara Chaube.

In the years that followed, the Chaube family treated Madana-gopala as one of their own, practising a spontaneous, unorthodox form of worship that was nonethless greatly relished hy the Lord.

Damodara Chaube’s wife, also a great devotee, served Madana-gopala as if she were His mother. She already had a son called Sadana, but she loved both boys equally, making no distinction between them.

Sadana would play with the boys of Mathura on Yamuna’s hanks, and sometimes Madana-gopala would accompany him, taking the form of a brahmana boy to play ball and other childish games.

When Rupa Gosvami and Sanatana Gosvami first came to Vrndavana, they would beg their meals (madhukari) in Mathura. On one such occasion, Sanatana knocked on the Chaubes’ door.

Upon seeing the form of Madana-gopala in the Chaubes’ house, Sanatana was enchanted. He left after a short time, but Sanatana continued to remember the Lord even after returning to Vrndavana.

Day and night Madana-gopala absorbed Sanatana’s mind, so much so that the saint would regularly appear at the Chaubes’ door for madhukari. Of course bis real purpose was to see the Deity.

On one occasion Sanatana Gosvami humbly instructed Chaube’s wife in the proper etiquette of worshipping the Deity on the path of regulated service, a form of worship befitting the Supreme Lord.

The brahmani immediately accepted Sanatana Gosvami’s advice and inaugurated the rules of Deity worship the saint bad given. She forbid her son Sadana to eat, play, or sleep with Madana-gopala.

That night Madana-gopala appeared in the dreams of both Sanatana Gosvami and Damodara Chaube. The Lord instructed each of them differently, but expressed to both His desire for a new arrangement.

To Sanatana, Madana-gopala said, “Mother Chaube loved Me and her son equally, but on your advice she now distinguishes between the two of us, worshipping Me with rituals and keeping Me at a distance.”

To Chaube, Madana-gopala said, “I have been with you lor a long time, but now I want to live with Sanatana Gosvami. You have Sadana, but Sanatana has no one. Please give Me to him right away.”

The next day Sanatana went to apologise to Chaube, but he was greeted with the news that Madana-gopala wanted to accept the service of Sanatana in Vrndavana. The Gosvamis bliss was boundless.

Sanatana Gosvami took Madana-gopala back to Dvadasaditya-tila where at first he worshipped the Lord beneath a tree, and then some time later in a thatched hut next to his bhajana-kutira.

Every day Sanatana Gosvami would beg some flour, bake little balls of dough in an open fire, boil some vegetables he had collected from the surrounding forests, and offer this to the Lord.

But Madana-gopala was used to a different kind of fare; Mother Chaube would cook with ghee and fine spices and give Gopala many preparations complemented hy the best quality rice available.

One day the Lord asked, “Every day you are offering Me these tasteless meals. Can’t you at least add a little salt?” Sanatana was pained to hear these words, but he was also reluctant to comply.

“I am Your eternal servant,” he answered, “but if I get You salt today, tomorrow You may ask for ghee. If I spend my days begging for food and cooking Your meals, how will I fulfill the orders of Mahaprabhu?”

Taking another capati-ball and placing it into Madana-gopala’s outstretched hand, Sanatana concluded, “Please accept what I have. And if You want more, please make Your own arrangements.”

This interaction between the Lord and His devotee cannot be imitated; we should not think that the standard of Deity worship can be lowered because it causes us inconvenience.

Madana-gopala was testing Sanatana Gosvami. In the form of a Deity, Madana-gopala was the Supreme Personality of Godhead, and in the form of Lord Caitanya He was Sanatana’s spiritual master.

This pastime shows that if one is advanced enough to receive direct orders from the Lord, if those orders happen to conflict with those of the spiritual master, the order of guru comes first. That pleases Krsna.

Madana-gopala accepted Sanatana Gosvami’s decision, but He still wanted salt and better accommodation, and so He arranged for those things without compromising His devotee’s primary services.

One day, a merchant named Rama Dasa Kapoor was taking three boatloads of merchandise — including salt — down the Yamuna to Agra when his boats got stuck on the sand flats of Kaliya-ghata.

Despite all efforts made by Rama Dasa and his sailors, the boats would not move. At that time Madana-gopala was playing nearby with some Vraja-vasi boys. In the form of a gopa, the Lord advised Kapoor.

Madana-gopala said, “On top of that hill lives a great saint. He will surely help you.” Hearing this, Rama Dasa Kapoor climbed to the top of Dvadasaditya-tila and explained his plight to Sanatana Gosvami.

Always sympathetic to the distress of others, Sanatana Gosvami guided the merchant to Madana-gopala, who had again assumed His Deity form. Sanatana said, “Pray to the Lord and He will help you.”

Rama Dasa Kapoor replied, “It the Lord trees my boat, and I can get to Agra without being attacked by thieves, I will be extremely grateful. From my profits I will build Him a beautiful temple here.”

Soon after Rama Dasa spoke these words, a heavy rainstorm broke out and raised the water level high enough to free the boats. The overjoyed merchant reiterated his pledge and headed for Agra.

Rama Dasa Kapoor left a supply of salt for Madana-gopala’s meals. And, after selling all his wares in Agra, true to his word, he returned to construct the first temple in Vrndavana.

In 1573 Emperor Akbar himself had given Jiva Gosvami permission to construct four temples in Vrndavana: one for Madana-gopala, the others for Govindadji, Gopinatha, and Yugala-kisora.

Rama Dasa Kapoor completed Madana-gopala’s temple in approximately 1580 and also donated a sizable agricultural plot to provide the temple and its pujaris with sufficient produce.

News of Madana-gopala’s and Govindadji’s appearance spread to the Vaisnava communities in Bengal and Orissa. Pilgrims soon began coming to see Their transcendental forms.

We should note that in those days transportation was provided solely by one’s own two feet. The trip to Vrndavana from Mayapur or Purirook many months of walking, with many hazards along the way.

Despite such hardships, devotees began travelling annually to Vraja for Parikrama, just as they had travelled to Puri to visit Caitanya Mahaprabhu and participate in the annual Ratha-yatra festival.

Of the nine processes of devotional service, visiting holy places and performing Parikrama are included in pada-sevanam. Vaisnavas serious about their spiritual practices never neglect such duties.

Now that devotees from Hungary and beyond can easily travel to New Vraja-dhama and serve Krsna’s land in relative comfort, we should make the most of this opportunity to enrich our spiritual lives.

**\* \* \* \***

The son of Maharaja Prataparudra, Purusottama Jana, heard that Govinda and Madana-gopala were being worshipped without consorts and wanted to render personal service to the two Lords.

He consulted with devotees in Puri and sent a well-guarded caravan carrying two Deities of Srimati Radharani to Vrndavana, along with cloth, ornaments, and other valuable items of worship.

Overjoyed at the arrival of the two Goddesses, Vrndavana’s Vaisnavas made grand arrangements for festivals at both temples, but Madana-gopala— in His playful way — personally changed those plans.

Madana-gopala appeared to His pujari in a dream and declared, “The two Deities just arrived from Purusottama-ksetra are not both Radha. The larger one is Lalita. Both should be installed with Me.”

News of Madana-gopala’s order spread throughout Vrndavana. The Vaisnavas were overjoyed. They placed the smaller Deity, Radha, to the Lord’s left and the larger one, Lalita, to His right.

Cakravarti Thakura prays,

*tarani ja-tira-bhuvi tarani-kara-varaka-priyaka-sandastha mani-sadana-mahita-sthite*  
*lalitaya sardham anupada-ramita radhaya madana-gopala nija-sadanam anuraksa mam*

**“O Madana-gopala, cowherd-hoy enchanter of Cupid! You happily reside in a splendid, jewelled palace within a shady grove of kadamba trees above the bank of the river Yamuna.**

**“As You and Srimati Radharani give pleasure to each other, Lalita-devi worships You with every atom of every breath she takes. ? Lord! May I also reside near Your temple to assist in serving You?”**

We have already heard how Sanatana Gosvami, in order to fully devote himself to the mission of Caitanya Mahaprabhu, turned over the worship of Madana-gopala to Krsna Dasa Brahmacari.

Establishing temples was certainly part of Sanatana Cosvami’s mission, but while others could carry out the temple worship, no one but Sanatana could write the books he had been empowered to write.

After installing Madana-mohana, Sanatana Gosvami spent much of his time at Radha-kunda, Pavana-sarovara, Gokula, and Manasa- ganga, writing, chanting, and otherwise spreading Krsna’s glories.

Sanatana and Madana-mohana may have been separated by distance, but the Deity always kept a watchful eye on Sanatana Gosvami to ensure that he was protected and his basic needs were provided for.

Once, at Nandagrama, the villagers who regularly brought milk to Sanatana Gosvami failed to come, and so Madana-mohana, in the guise of a local cowherd boy, personally brought milk to His devotee.

In one well-known pastime, a cowherd boy gave the elderly Sanatana a Govardhana-sila to circumambulate instead of Govardhana Hill. That boy revealed Himself to be none other than Madana-mohana.

These touching exchanges of love continued right up to the time of Sanatana Gosvai’s demise. Some devotees were fortunate enough to witness them, and thus we are privileged to hear about them today.

I have extensively described the pastimes of Madana-mohana to illustrate how responsive the Deity is to the love of His pure devotee, and to inspire us to seek out such loving exchanges for ourselves.

Sentient living entities are all eager for significant, dynamic loving relationships. For devotees, those relationships can be actively cultivated with Krsna through the tangible form of the Deity.

Incidents such as those between Madana-gopala and Sanatana are not mere stories. Such things can happen at any time to anyone whose faith and devotion are mature enough to attract the Lord.

It is sometimes said that Deity worship is for neophytes. But once, when Jagannatha Dasa Babaji had to give up the Deities in his care, he said, “I cannot live without Deities. Please find me others.”

Lord Kapiladeva specifically teaches His mother about the interaction between the Deity of Krsna and the worshipper. He says, “O My mother! My devotees always see the smiling face of My form —

“With eyes like the rising morning sun. They like to see My various transcendental \[Deity\] forms, which are all benevolent, and they also talk favourably with Me.

“Upon seeing the charming forms of the Supreme Lord, smiling and attractive, and hearing His very pleasing words, the pure devotee almost loses all other consciousness.

“His senses are freed from all other engagements, and he becomes absorbed in devotional service. Thus in spite of his unwillingness, he attains liberation without separate endeavour.”

Srila Prahhupada’s purports explain that while only advanced devotees converse with the Deity, even neophytes’ faithful service to the Lord yields a sense of liberation that gradually leads to perfection.

I le writes, “The devotees express their minds before the Deity, and in many instances the Deity also gives answers. But one must be a very elevated devotee in order to be able to speak with the Supreme Lord.

“Sometimes the Lord informs the devotee through dreams. These exchanges of feelings between the Deity and the devotee are not understandable by atheists, but actually the devotee enjoys them.”

“In other words, it is confirmed herewith that even a third-class devotee is in the transcendental position, above those who are trying for liberation by speculation or by other methods.

“Even the third-class devotee — who...offers obeisances with great devotion, thinks of the Lord, sees the Lord in the temple, and brings flowers and fruits to the Deity — becomes imperceptibly liberated.”

Thus we can rest assured that Radha-Syamasundara are as willing and as able to intimately respond to our devotion as was Madana-mohana, provided we follow Sanatana Gosvamis teachings and example.

To show that Madana-mohana reciprocated with devotees other than Sanatana Gosvami, I conclude this section with a story that took place after the Lord had left Vraja to settle in Karauli, Rajasthan.

In 1670, Madana-mohana displayed the pastime of fleeing Emperor Aurangzeb’s persecution and wandering for almost sixty years until He settled in Karauli at King Gopala Singh’s request.

Some years later, during the reign of Maharaja Harikaspal, an old devotee woman lived in a village near Karauli. Her sole source of income was selling milk from her small herd of cows.

Every day she would go to Karauli. On the way she would thin her milk with water from a creek she had to ford. At Karauli, before going to market, she would offer a cup of milk to Madana-mohana.

Once, as she was thinning the milk with creek water, a small fish swam unnoticed into her pot, and from there into the cup of milk she gave to Madana-mohana’s pujari to offer to the Lord.

Just as the pujari was about to make the offering, he noticed the tiny fish. He at once rejected the milk, scolded the woman, and forbade her to make any further offerings to the Deities.

The old gopi was heartbroken that her only service to Madana-mohana had been taken away from her. She returned home, where she cried and fasted, continually praying to the Lord for forgiveness.

Determined to somehow regain her service to the Lord, she prayed and prayed to Madana-mohana for guidance, hoping to find out how she could rectify herself and regain His favour.

Madana-mohana took pity on the old gopi. In the guise of a mendicant He came knocking on her door late that night and requested a place to stay until the following morning.

The gopi asked, “Boy! Where do you come from? I have not seen you in these parts before.” The mendicant answered, “Mother! I live in Vraja and have come to take darsana of Madana-mohana.

“Please allow me to stay the night. Tomorrow I will go to Karaull.” The old lady felt obliged to accommodate the saint, who was not only young but also had a compellingly familiar countenance.

“Babaji!” the elderly cowherd woman replied. “My house is yours. Tell me, what can I offer you to eat?” The sage replied, “I only drink milk. If you can offer me a little, then I will accept that.”

The old woman only had the milk in which the fish had been trapped, and so she offered to go and milk her cow. But the young mendicant said, “No! I can’t wait! Give me what you have now.”

The gopi explained that there had been a fish in the milk, hut the young man replied, “In the future you should sieve your milk when diluting it, hut for now I am too hungry to wait, please give it as it is.”

Unable to change the young mendicant’s mind, the old woman gave him the milk. After drinking it with obvious satisfaction, he said, “Mother! You know your milk is very tasty — the best I have drunk.”

The young man then lay down on a cot and fell asleep, but the old woman, still tormented by her offence to Madana-mohana, was unable to sleep. She stayed awake most of the night.

The air became chilly after midnight, and so the gopi covered her guest with an old quilt. Finally, she went to her room to rest. Towards the end of night she drifted off and dreamt of Madana-mohana.

In her dream the Lord smiled kindly and said, “Why are you still fasting, thinking that I have refused your milk? Don’t you know that I have come to your house and taken milk directly from your hands?

“Continue your service,” Madana-mohana concluded. “The pujari will not object any further. Now get up and eat.” The gopi's dream broke and she rose to find that the young sadhu had disappeared.

She approached his cot and saw that her old quilt had been exchanged for Madana-mohana’s fine yellow silk shawl. At once she understood that the young sadhu had been none other than the Lord.

Overwhelmed with various symptoms of ecstasy, it took the old woman time to compose herself. She then followed Madana-mohana’s orders, ate a little, bathed, milked her cows and left for Karauli.

Meanwhile, the pujari had gone to wake Radha Madana-mohana and found the Lord wearing a torn quilt instead of His opulent shawl. The brahmana could not understand what had happened.

Indeed, none of the Deities’ servants could account for the absence of Madana-mohana’s shawl, or the presence of the quilt, until the old gopi arrived with the yellow shawl the Lord had worn the day before.

The old woman handed the shawl to the pujari and said, “Madana- mohana came to my house last night to drink milk. It was dark when He left. He must have mistaken my quilt for His shawl.’’

Madana-mohana’s servants were at a loss for words, but they could not contest the evidence of the shawl and the quilt. They concluded that, no matter what, the Lord would accept the old gopis milk.

**\* \* \* \***

In the corner of Dvadasaditya-tila, where Nitai Circular Road intersects Varsana Road, is Kaliyadamana-vana, marking the forest that commemorates Krsna’s triumph over the hundred-headed snake.

The house there, called Nanda-bhavana, represents the samadhi of Prabodhananda Sarasvati Thakura, author of Sri Vrndavana-mahimamrta and the uncle and guru of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami.

This Nanda-bhavana is not connected to Nanda Maharaja. It marks a place of forgiveness in Vraja, where pilgrims can get relief from reactions to sinful deeds they performed since their last pilgrimage.

I he residents of this house keep a stick made from the wood of a tree that grew in New Vraja-dhama. Sincerely repentant pilgrims are thrice struck ceremonially with this stick and their sins are forgiven.

A dearth of information about Prabodhananda Sarasvati Thakura makes it impossible to give a clear chronological picture of his life, his years in Vrndavana, or even of all his literary achievements.

Kavi-karnapura identifies him as the learned gopi Tungavidya in Krsna’s pastimes, and indirectly refers to his Caitanya-candramrta hy writing, “...he eloquently glorified the qualities of Lord Caitanya.”

Srila Prabhupada often quotes Prabodhananda Sarasvati. In fact, one of His Divine Grace’s favourite quotes regarding flattery in preaching comes from Caitanya-candramrta, which he paraphrases as follows:

**“I take a straw in my mouth. I fall down at your feet and flatter you. ‘O sir! You are such a great and intelligent man. There is no one greater than you. You are so intelligent, rich, and beautiful.**

**“‘You may ask, ‘Why are you so humble? Why are you flattering me?What is your intention? Tell me.’ And I reply, ‘O great sadhu! I know you have learned so many nice things, but forget them, please.**

**‘“Whatever nonsense you have learned, whatever hogwash is in your brain, throw it all out. Throw it all far away and place all your intelligence, all your love in the lotus feet of Lord Gauranga.”**

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura calls Prabodhananda Sarasvati the “king of tridandi sannyasis following in the footsteps of Lord Caitanya," and elsewhere glorifies him in the following way:

**“The very same mercy and teachings that Tridandi Prabhu Sri Prabodhananda received from Sriman Mahaprabhu were preserved by him in verses for the benefit of future devotees.**

**“Those who have a taste for the mood of Prabodhananda are worthy of being called Vaisnavas, otherwise one simply wanders about searching tor eatables like banana stalks, dahi halls, and drumsticks."**

Thakura Bhaktivinoda also explained Prabodhananda Sarasvati’s mission:

**“\[He\] was given the task of showing the world that the path of the rasas and anubhavas in Vraja is the highest ot all paths.”**

These references from our recent acaryas affirm Prabodhananda Sarasvati’s authenticity and relevance to the Gaudiya Vaisnava mission, even though his contemporaries hardly mentioned him.

Sri Caitanya-caritamrta does not mention his name, but does recount Lord Caitanya’s four-month stay in the home ot Prabodhananda Sarasvati’s elder brother Trimalla Bhatta at Sri-ranga-ksetra.

Bhakti-ratnakara is more specific. It affirms that Prabodhananda and his other elder brother, Venkata Bhatta, served and associated with Caitanya Mahaprabhu during the entire period of His stay.

Sanatana Gosvami refers to Prabodhananda Sarasvati as the “beloved of Krsna” (bhagavata-priya), and Radha-krsna Gosvami confirms that he was the uncle and guru of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami.

Considering the significance of his spiritual identity and his contribution to Gaudiya Vaisnava tradition, that the Gosvamis neither wrote of nor quoted Prabodhananda Sarasvati is a mystery.

In any case, little is known of the Thakura’s activities in Vrndavana, including the date of his demise. It is commonly understood that his samadhi marks the place where he regularly engaged in bhajana.

Because so little is known of Prabodhananda Sarasvati, misconceptions about him persist. For example, he is often misidentified as the Mayavadi Prakasananda Sarasvati of Varanasi.

What’s more, the authorship of his Radha-rasa-sudha-nidhi is sometimes attributed to a follower of Gopala Bhatta, Hit Harivamsa, who was excommunicated from the Gaudiya Vaisnava community.

Srila Prabhupada disproves that the two Sarasvatis — Prabodhananda and Prakasananda— are the same person. He also clearly states that Prabodhananda is the author of Radha-rasa-sudha-nidhi.

Before continuing my Parikrama, I recited the one prayer I know about Prabodhananda Sarasvati Thakura,

*parama-vairagya-sneha murti manoram*  
*maha-kavi gita vadya-nrtye anupam*

**“Very renounced, Prabodhananda Sarasvati overflowed with love. He was handsome, a learned poet and scholar, and had no equal in the arts of singing, dancing, and playing musical instruments.”**

Behind Kaliyadamana-vana is Gendokhara-ksetra. The cowherd boys would sometimes play ball there. Two kundas mark where Krsna and Balarama stood, one to the north and one to the south, respectively.

The boys play ball often, and Krsna invents many novel games to enjoy with His friends. He often becomes so absorbed in these games that He forgets to eat, rest, or even to go home.

There is a special charm in Krsna’s ball games that, like the rasa dance, elevates them into a category of “beautiful pastimes” as differentiated from pastimes like flute playing or milking cows.

Sometimes the boys play football with round fruits like bael or amalaki, and sometimes they play catch, throwing unripe fruits back and forth to one another while walking through the forest.

With balls made of wood or stuffed and stitched hide, the gopas play different games using wooden bats. If His bat is broken, Krsna requests His father to have a new one made.

In another game the boys roll clusters of kunda flowers into halls and then throw them at one another. This sport so thrills ihe boys that their laughter can be heard all over Vraja.

At Gendokhara-ksetra the boys make a wide circle and throw a ball to one another. Whoever drops the ball has to leave the game. The two lakes there mark where Krsna and Balarama stand.

Intent on intercepting a ball thrown over His head, sometimes Krsna holds His turban in place with His left hand, looks up, and deftly catches the ball with His right, calling out, “Got it!”

And when He wants to throw the ball so fast that it cannot be caught, He gracefully bends His arm, drawing His fist behind His car, and then throws the ball with lightning speed.

Some boys are unable to field Krsna’s speedballs, but expert players like Devaprastha can easily catch these throws, often mocking Krsna by saying things like “Gopala! Is that the best You can do?”

These games of ball go on continuously at Gendokhara-ksetra. Krsna plays and plays, His curly hair flying everywhere, until His body is completely covered with Vrndavana’s dust and His perspiration.

Only serious fatigue can make Him stop. He then rests in the shelter of a tree, lying upon a friend’s shawl as another boy fans Him with leaves and yet another massages His calves and feet.

Narottama Dasa Thakura prays to enter Vraja in order to watch these sports of Krsna and His friends. And when the gopas return home, Narottama hopes to smear the dust of their feet all over his body.

On the eastern side of the Parikrama path, across from Gendokhara-ksetra, is Astasakhi-vana, named after the temple of the asta-sakhis, the eight main gopi friends of Radha and Krsna.

Bhaktisiddhanta Sarasvati Thakura’s disciple, Raja Ramranjan, whose wife had a dream in which Krsna requested that a temple be built for the glorification of these sakhis, built the original temple in Vraja.

In New Vraja-dhama, surrounding a picture of Radha-Syama, pictures of the asta-sakhis— Lalita, Visakha, Citra, Campakalata, Sudevi, Rangadevi, Indulekha, and Tungavidya — are worshipped here.

**\* \* \* \***

Just south of here, on the same side of the road as Astasakhivana, is the samadhi of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, whom I have already written about in connection with the temple of Radha-ramana.

Gopala Bhatta was horn in 1500 near Sri Ranga as the son of Venkata Bhatta, an aristocratic South Indian brahmana from the Ramanuja sampradaya who was entrusted with the care of Ranganatha.

In 1511, Lord Caitanya spent the four-month rainy season with Gopala’s family. The Lord transformed Prabodhananda into a great Vaisnava and made Gopala mad for Him and for Radha and Krsna.

Venkata and Trimalla Bhatta were both worshippers of Lord Narayana, while Prabodhananda was inclined to monistic philosophy in his youth. But Gopala was a devotee of Krsna from birth.

*Kavi-karnapura discloses the acaryas eternal spiritual identity*

**“Ananga-manjari appeared as Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, although some say he was the incarnation of Guna-manjari."**

Gopala was only eleven years old when he served Lord Caitanya in his father’s house; still, he lamented that he had been unable to see Sri Caitanya in Navadvipa when the Lord was si ill a householder.

Gopala’s sorrow was clearly evident: his eyes were always full of tears, and his actions reflected the ecstasy of divine madness. To pacify the boy, Caitanya Mahaprabhu appeared to him in a dream.

In the dream Gopala saw Navadvipa, with its palm trees standing tall along the Ganges. Then he saw Nimai Pandita, the enchanter of Cupid, walking gracefully down the river road like a young elephant.

Lord Nityananda and Advaita Prabhu, amongst others, walked with the Lord as He chanted the names of Radha and Krsna, occasionally casting glances at Gopala, who was also present in the assembly.

Lord Nityananda was about to embrace Gopala, but the young brahmana awoke. Still in the bewilderment of divine ecstasy, he left his room in search of Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu was in His own quarters, waiting for Gopala to arrive. As the boy opened the door, the Lord assumed His form as a cowherd boy with a blue complexion, playing on a flute.

Suddenly the Lord’s blue complexion changed. Gopala saw the same golden form he had seen while dreaming, with black curly hair falling to the Lord’s shoulders, His forehead marked with Vaisnava tilaka.

The Lord’s eyebrows were curved like a bow, His soothing glance was more than adequate to steal the hearts of women, His face outshone an autumn moon, and His soft smile exuded rays of sweetness.

The Lord wore a beautiful dhoti, expertly draped in three folds around His body. From His neck hung a malati garland, and His limbs, which moved with transfixing grace, were decorated with ornaments.

Gopala fell at the feet of Lord Caitanya in His form as Nimai Pandita. But as Gopala raised his head expectantly, his eyes met the smiling face of the Lord’s ascetic form, with shaven head and saffron cloth.

At the end of the rainy season, before Caitanya Mahaprabhu left to continue His walking tour, He instructed Gopala to stay with his parents for as long as they needed him and then go to Vraja.

The Lord said, “There you will meet Rupa and Sanatana. They will reveal to you the mysteries of My truths and moods. In this way you will understand everything about Radha and Krsna in Vraja.”

As the Lord was leaving, He told Venkata Bhatta: “Gopala has My blessings. Make sure he gets a good education, but do not get him married.” Gopala Bhatta would never see the Lord again.

Nevertheless, Gopala was always rapt in thoughts of Lord Caitanya, and the Lord was always thinking of Gopala Bhatta. In fact, the boy’s great devotion and purity made him the pride of all Sri Ranga.

Gopala Bhatta not only became a greatly learned scholar, defeating local Mayavadis, but also became skilled in music, singing, dance, and even drama, showing that a devotee is indeed expert in everythin”.

As soon as Gopala Bhatta’s parents died, he went straight to Vrndavana and took shelter of Rupa and Sanatana. But Lord Caitanya, having heard no news of him, was anxious about Gopala.

One day in Puri, while the Lord and His devotees were honouring prasada at the house of Gadadhara Pandita, Mahaprabhu said, “I am concerned. I have not received news from Vraja for quite some time.”

As the Lord was speaking, a letter came from Rupa and Sanatana announcing that Gopala had arrived safely in Vrndavana. Overjoyed to hear this, the Lord glorified Gopala to all the devotees there.

He recounted His stay at the house of Venkata Bhatta, and told how Gopala, even as a young boy, was pure in heart, fully devoted to Him, and always extremely eager to serve Krsna.

Lord Caitanya left His meal and immediately composed a letter for Rupa and Sanatana. He sent it to Vrndavana along with pieces of His clothing and His cauki in a show of great affection for His devotees.

After the appearance of Radha-ramana, Gopala Bhatta started to accept disciples on the order of Rupa and Sanatana, who preferred to emphasise their succession through their instructions (siksa).

It is interesting to note that the senior Gosvamis decided not to initiate disciples; instead, they appointed two devotees from whom aspirants could take diksa: Gopala Bhatta and Raghunatha Bhatta.

Gopala Bhatta Gosvami was empowered to initiate those who came from the western side of India, while Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami would initiate all the devotees who came from the area of Gaudadesa.

Two of Gopala Bhatta’s most prominent disciples were Damodara and Gopinatha. The former became a grhastha and received the right to worship Radha-ramana. The latter remained a renunciant.

Gopala Bhatta’s most famous and prominent disciple was Srinivasa Acarya, who was directed hy Sri Jiva Gosvami to accept diksa from Gopala Bhatta, even though Srinivasa was from Bengal.

In 1586, one hundred years after Lord Caitanya’s appearance, Gopala Bhatta passed away at the age of eighty-five. His samadhi is near the site where Radha-ramana appeared from Gopala’s Salagrama-sila.

The major literary contributions of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami arc his Hari-bhakti-vilasa and the outline for the Sandarbhas that Jiva Gosvami later organised and elaborated upon.

Jiva Gosvami writes,

**“One friend of theirs \[of Rupa’s and Sanatana’s\], a Bhatta scholar from a family of South Indian brahmanas, compiled this book after carefully studying the writings of venerable Vaisnavas.**

**“Now, Jiva, having noticed that while some portions of this text were in proper order, others were not, and still others were missing, will write it out in the right order after proper deliberation.”**

Remembering Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, let us pray, “O crest jewel of Vaisnavas, whose mind always dwells upon the pastimes of Radha- ramana, and who is always overwhelmed in bliss.

“O Vaisnava Thakura! You are the friend of the fallen. Your name, as a transcendental philosophers’ stone, is spread throughout the three worlds. O master! Just once allow me to see your lotus feet.”

**\* \* \* \***

The next house marks the samadhi of Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami, the illustrious son of Tapana Misra, friend of Rupa and Sanatana, and Vrndavana’s favourite reciter of Srimad-Bhagavatam.

In Bhauma Vrndavana Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami’s ashes are in the sixty-four samadhis area, but because he is one of the Six Gosvamis, his samadhi has been given its own place in New Vraja-dhama.

Raghunatha was favoured by the Lord since their first meeting in Varanasi. After Raghunatha stayed for eight months in Puri, Lord Caitanya sent him back to Varanasi to care for his elderly parents.

Before Raghunatha Bhatta left Puri, Lord Caitanya told him not t? marry. To give the young brahmana strength and inspiration, the Lord presented Raghunatha with His own neck beads kantha-mala.

It is important to note that Lord Caitanya asked even His most intimate associates Gopala Bhatta and Raghunatha Bhatta, important generals in His mission, to care for their devotee parents.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu told both young men not to marry, but He did not allow them to abandon their obligation to their parents; rather, He expected them to care for their superiors until they died.

Only after their parents had left this world did Caitanya Mahaprabhu allow these devotees to take up their duties in His movement, helping to spread Krsna consciousness all over the world.

If we find ourselves in such a situation, in which our aging parents— especially if they are devotees — have no one else to care for them, we have the Lord’s instructions as a guideline for what to do.

Four years later, when his parents left this world for Goloka, Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami returned to Jagannatha Puri and stayed for another eight months, servingCaitanya Mahaprabhu.

Amongst his many devotional accomplishments, Raghunatha Bhatta was an expert cook. He often invited Lord Caitanya and His associales and served them dishes that rivalled the taste of nectar.

Raghunatha Bhatta was a great scholar, having been trained in Varanasi, one of the world’s foremost seats of learning at that time. It was there that he intently studied Srimad-Bhagavatam.

While in Puri, Raghunatha had the opportunity to hear from the Lord’s associates, especially from Gadadhara Pandita, who would regularly speak from Srimad-Bhagavatam to an assembly of devotees.

One day Caitanya Mahaprabhu called Raghunatha Bhatta to His side and said, “My dear Raghunatha! On My order, go to Vrndavana and live there under the care of Rupa Gosvami and Sanatana Gosvami.’”

*“Bhagavata pada, sada laila krsna-nama*  
*acire karibena krpa krsna bhagavan”*  
*eta bali prabhu tanre alingana kaila*  
*prabhura krpate krsna-preme matta haila*

**‘“In Vrndavana you should chant the Hare Krsna mantra twenty-four hours a day and read Srimad-Bhagavatam continuously. Krsna, the Supreme Lord, will very soon hestow His mercy upon you.’**

**“After saying this, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu embraced Raghunatha Bhatta, and hy the Lord’s mercy Raghunatha was enlivened with ecstatic love for Krsna.”**

To pacify Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvamis aching heart, Sri Caitanya Mahaprabhu gave him the remnants of Lord Jagannatha in the form of a long tulasi garland and spiced betel nuts and leaves.

Rupa Gosvami and Sanatana Gosvami received Raghunatha Bhatta in Vrndavana with affection, giving him a suitable place to live in an area that now lies within the precincts of Govindadji’s temple.

Lord Caitanya’s purpose in sending Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami t? Vrndavana was to have him hold public discourses on Srimad-Bhagavatam, just as Gadadhara Pandita was doing in Jagannatha Puri.

In establishing His teachings, Lord Caitanya did not introduce any new texts, but rather emphasised Srimad-Bhagavatam as the basis of His sankirtana movement and as the topmost scripture.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu taught His followers that Srimad-Bhagavatam— like the Deity and the maha-mantra — is not different from Krsna; hearing it puts one in intimate contact with the Lord.

By the Lord’s grace, Raghunatha Bhatta was famous for his Bhagavatam recitations. Even Rupa and Sanatana would attend. They were spell bound by the acarya s expressions of love for Krsna.

Whenever Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami spoke, he would wear the tulasi garland given to him by the Lord. He would recite each verse three or four times, each time in a different tune.

As he spoke of the beauty and sweetness of Krsna, he would often be overwhelmed by ecstatic love. Faltering of the voice, trembling, and tears would sometimes force him to end his recitation.

By the order of Rupa and Sanatana, Raghunatha Bhatta accepted the service of initiating disciples. Many prominent persons became his disciples, including Emperor Akbar’s finance minister, Todar Mal.

Raghunatha was sold out, heart and soul, to Govindadji. He prepared beautiful ornaments for the Deity, like a flute or earrings, and he would always speak Srimad-Bhagavatam in Govinda’s presence.

At that time Govindadji was worshipped in a small, humble temple, but Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami was always meditating on how to build a grand palace worthy of Rupa Gosvamis Lord.

Whenever influential disciples asked how to serve him, the acarya would request them to construct a suitable temple for Govinda. Though the Gosvami did not live to see it, his order was carried out.

Government records confirm that Todar Mal persuaded Akbar to officially recognise the custodians of the temples of Govinda and Madana-mohana, a monumental achievement under Muslim rule.

Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami later took up residence at Radha-kunda, where he continued his Srimad-Bhagavatam discourses and from where he ultimately entered the transcendental realm.

Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami, Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami, and Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami all left this world on the same day—the Asvina Sukla-dvadasi, just before Karttika — though in different years.

Completely absorbed in thoughts of Sri Krsna, Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami displayed the qualities of a first-class devotee who saw everyone as a servant of Krsna. He was above petty criticism.

In glorification of Raghunatha Bhatta, Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami wrote the following: “He would not listen to blasphemy of a Vaisnava, nor would he listen to talk of a Vaisnava’s misbehaviour.”

According to Kavi-karnapura, the gopi called Raga-manjari in Krsna’s pastimes appeared as Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami.Srinivasa Acarya, on a visit to Raghunatha Bhatta’s samadhi, had this to say:

**“As I look upon the samadhi of Raghunatha Bhatta, my heart breaks and my eyes are awash with tears, but as soon as I remember Raghunatha Bhatta’s glories, I am overjoyed. Who could not be?**

**“He was such an expert teacher of all the scriptures that even Brhaspati would joyfully cheer upon hearing Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami’s explanations of Gaudiya Vaisnava doctrine.**

**“There is nothing to which his discourses on Srimad-Bhagavatam can he compared. Even Vyasa himself wished to sit and listen to him speak, knowing that doing so would bring him happiness.”**

Dear devotees! Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvamis dedication to explaining Srimad-Bhagavatam indicates that his samadhi should be a place where that divine text is recited constantly.

In the house after Raghunatha Bhatta’s samadhi, Radha-Syamasundara are worshipped as Banke-bihari, a Deity discovered in Nidhuvana by Svami Haridasa during the time of the Six Gosvamis.

Banke-bihari reflects Syamasundara’s casual threefold-bending form (banka), which indicates the Supreme Personality of Godhead's natural proclivity for, and exclusive way of enjoying (bihari).

One of Vraja’s main festivals for Banke-bihari is Jhulana-yatra, in which the Lord is offered a different kind of swing each day. On the third day of the waxing moon, He is given a swing made of gold.

East of Banke-bihari temple is an open field. It represents one that was a playground for Krsna and the cowherd boys, who indulged in all kinds of games (krta) there. The field is thus known as Gopakrta.

The entire area, from Gopakrta to the samadhi of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, marks Biharivana, a place that is home to a playground for Syama, a temple for His service, and a final resting place for acaryas.

May our minds and hearts remain always absorbed in service to and association with these great souls, for their mercy is true spiritual sustenance and will be our key to entrance into eternal Vrndavana.

In this regard, Narottama Dasa Thakura sings,

*sri-rupa sanatana bhatta-raghunatha*  
*sri-jiva gopala-bhatta dasa-raghunatha*  
*ei chay gosair kari carana vandan*  
*jaha hoite bighna-nas abhista-puran*

**“All glories to Sri Rupa Gosvami, Sanatana Gosvami, Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami, Sri Jiva Gosvami, Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, and to Raghunatha Dasa Gosvami.**

**“I offer my respectful obeisances to the feet of these Six Gosvamis. By bowing to their lotus feet, all obstacles to devotion are destroyed and all spiritual desires are fulfilled.**

*Ei chay gosai jar-mia tar das*  
*ta-sabara pada-renu morn panca-gras*  
*tadera carana-sebi-bhakta-sane bas*  
*janame janame hoy ei abhilas*  
*ei chay gosai jabe braje koila bas*  
*radha-krsna-nitya-lila karila prakas*

**“I am the servant of that person who is a servant of these Six Gosvamis of Vrndavana. The dust of their lotus feet is for me like the five kinds of food. This is my desire:**

**“Birth after birth may I live with those who serve the lotus feet of these Gosvamis. When the Six Gosvamis lived in Vraja they revealed lost holy places and explained the pastimes of Radha and Krsna.”**

The acarya concludes hy encouraging us in this way,

*anande bolo hari bhaja brndaban*  
*sri-guru-vaisnaba-pade majaiya man*  
*sri-guru-vaisnaba-pada-padma kori as*  
*narottama dasa kohe nama-sankirtana*

**“Just sing the names of Hari in ecstasy and worship the divine realm of Vrndavana while absorbing your mind in meditation upon the divine feet of the spiritual master and the Vaisnavas.**

**“Desiring to serve the lotus feet of Sri Guru and the Vaisnavas, Narottama Dasa Thakura sings this sankirtana of the transcendental names of Lord Hari.”**

*Once in Kevārivana, a demon friend of Kaṁsa started a ring of fire around Kṛṣṇa and the Vraja-vāsīs with the intention of killing them all. But Kṛṣṇa merely swallowed that inferno whole.*

## Chapter 22 - Khonha to Viharavana

All glories to the prince and princess of Vrndavana’s kings! They are the dual forms of the Absolute Truth, hut like lively children They join Their friends in playing kho-kho and other village games.

Kho-kho is a modified form of tag, which in its simplest form involves chasing and touching one’s opponents. Long ago, warriors on horsedrawn chariots played a heroic version of kho-kho called rathera.

All glories to Radha and Krsna. Inspired as much hy curiosity as by mutual affection, They spy on each other in the midst of playing childish games. Each relishes the sight of the other's abandon in play.

In New Vraja-dhama, the open space opposite the samadhi of Raghunatha Bhatta Gosvami represents the gopis’ playing field, called Khonha, where Srimati Radharani and Her friends played kho-kho.

The eastern corner of Khonha represents Vraja’s sacred Samadhivana, where samadhis of the sixty-four primary associates of Lord Caitanya (Causatti-mahanta-samadhi-pitha) can be found.

Many of those shrines are nama-samadhis, although Jiva Gosvami brought some of Bhatta Gosvami’s ashes from Radha-kunda and constructed a second samadhi for Raghunatha there.

In New Vraja-dhama, one shrine symbolises all sixty-four samadhis. By worshipping, circumamhulating, or bowing down here, we can offer respect to all the prominent associates of Caitanya Mahaprabhu.

Residents who walk hy ibis garden-forest, which borders Varsana Road and Sad-gosvami Cireular Road, can offer a single flower here to receive the benefit of offering flowers at all sixty-four samadhis.

Pilgrims who come for even a short visit to New Vraja-dhama and would like to offer their respects to the Lord’s associates can do so here and receive the transcendental blessings of our previous acaryas.

Just south of the samadhi-pitha is one of many venu-kupas in Vraja, wells that Krsna created to quench the thirst of His associates. Here He enacted that pastime again to satisfy one of His friends.

The water Krsna invokes with His flute is ambrosial and sometimes comes from special rivers like the Ganges. It is my firm conviction that the well water of New Vraja-dhama is specially blessed by Syama.

Ever since a well was dug outside the house where Radha-Gopinatha is honoured, I have drunk only its water during the times I have been living in New Vraja-dhama. The taste of the water is unique.

I have had many kinds of expensive, imported, naturally filtered mineral water, but none can compare to the silky smoothness, subtle sweetness, or devotional flavour of New Vraja — dhama's well water.

Biharivana stretches over to the other side of the road, encompassing Venu-kupa, Madhu Pandita’s samadhi, and the aforementioned temple of Radha-Gopinatha with its well of nectar.

Let us offer our respectful obeisances to Madhu Pandita, a disciple of Sri Gadadhara Pandita, a resident of Khadadaha, Bengal, and the servant of Gopinatha, the master of the gopis.

Madhu, like his illustrious spiritual master, Gadadhara Pandita, was a great scholar and proponent ot the scriptures, headed hy Srimad- Bhagavatam; consequently, he too was awarded the title “Pandita.”

While living in Jagannatha Puri, Madhu would hear about the qualities, forms, and pastimes of Lord Krsna. Knowing well that Krsna lives always in Vrndavana, he asked his guru’s permission to go there.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu had told Gadadhara Pandita to stay in Puri, so he was never able to go to Vraja. But when Gadadhara’s disciple requested permission to go, he was happy to give Madhu his blessings.

Madhu Pandita expected that as he toured the twelve forests of Vraja he would see Krsna at any moment, but the superior principle of service in separation dictated that he would not.

Two similar accounts tell how the Deity of Gopinatha came to Madhu Pandita. In one account, Paramananda Bhatta found the Deity, whereas in the other account Madhu Pandita found Gopinatha.

The first version tells that one year the rainy season was so intense, the swollen Yamuna forcefully uprooted the original Vamsivata tree beneath which Krsna had called the gopis with His flute.

Paramananda Bhatta, another disciple of Gadadhara Pandita, took a branch from that tree and, while trying to plant it at the original site, unearthed the ancient Deity of Gopinatha.

Paramananda Bhatta entrusted the worship of Gopinatha to Madhu Pandita, who was a close friend of his, and Madhu Pandita eventually inspired a patron to construct a temple just behind Vamsivata.

In the second version, on one rainy day at Vamsivata, as Madhu Pandita was lamenting in separation from Krsna, the Lord appeared before His devotee, fulfilling the Pandita’s long-cherished desire.

Madhu Pandita feasted his eyes on the transcendental form of the Lord and, after offering Him prayers, begged Krsna not to disappear again and leave him destitute, as the Lord had done before.

Krsna agreed to Madhu Pandita’s proposal and revealed that He was buried at that very spot in the form of His Deity, which Madhu then installed where Krsna had inaugurated the rasa dance long ago.

Both versions agree that Gopinatha was originally fashioned and installed by Vajra in Vrndavana and that Madhu Pandita served the Lord throughout his life. The date of his leaving remains unknown.

Sri Caitanya-caritamrta mentions that when the Gopala Deity was residing at Vitthalesvara’s house in Mathura, Madhu Pandita was among the devotees who accompanied Rupa Gosvami to see the Lord.

Madhu Pandita was also amongst the devotees who greeted Virabhadra Gosvami, the son of Lord Nityananda, when he visited Vraja. It was Madhu who took him for darsana of Radha-Gopinatha.

Bhakti-ratnakara recounts that Madhu Pandita gave the blessings of Gopinatha to Srinivasa Acarya to take the Gosvamis’ books to Bengal, and so Madhu must have lived past 1582.

In New Vraja-dhama, the samadhi of Madhu Pandita is near radha-Gopinatha temple, as it is in Bhauma Vrndavana, where the acaryas full-body samadhi is found by the temple entrance.

In radha-Gopinatha temple, we honour Radha-Syamasundara as the ultimate object of pure devotional service — He who delights in the company of beautiful young gopis headed by Sri Radha.

Thus we pray,

*sriman rasa-rasarambhi vamsivata-tata-sthitah*  
*karsan venu-svanair gopir gopi-nathah sriye ’stu nah*

**“Sri Srila Gopinatha, who originated the transcendental mellow of the rasa dance -**

**Stands on the shore in Vamsivata and attracts the attention of the cowherd damsels, the gopis, with the sound of His celebrated flute. May they all confer upon us their benediction.”**

One of Akbar’s generals, Rayasila Sekhavata, was once handsomely rewarded for winning a battle on behalf of the emperor. At Madhu Pandita’s behest, he then built the first temple of Gopinatha.

Some people assert that Gopinatha’s temple was built before Madana-mohana’s, but it is commonly accepted amongst Vraja’s authorities that Madana-mohana’s temple was the first to be completed in Vraja.

Aurangzeb’s soldiers desecrated the original Gopinatha temple. The present temple was constructed more than one hundred years later, and a set of pratibhu Deities are now worshipped there.

During Aurangzeb’s atrocities, Gopinatha and His entourage of gopis, along with many other original Deities of Vraja, gradually made Their way through Vraja and Rajasthan to Jaipur, where They reside today.

How did Radha and other gopis come to Gopinatha? Originally, Jahnava-devi sent a Deity of Radha to Vrndavana. Sri Radha was installed alongside Gopinatha in a glorious festival.

That Deity of Radha now stands on Gopinatha’s right. Where the first Radha once stood on His left, a second Deity was later installed by Jahnava-devi. Why does Krsna have two consorts?

During one of Jahnava-devi’s visits to Vrndavana, she had the thoughi that the first Radha was too small in proportion to Gopinatha, and that a larger Deity would be more appropriate.

She didn’t say anything, however, but just returned to her home after arati. That night, Gopinatha came to her in a dream and exclaimed that Radha was indeed too small and looked odd by His side.

He requested that Jahnava arrange for a new Deity of Radha to be prepared in Gaudadesa and placed on His left side, with the smaller Radha on His right. But Jahnava doubted this instruction.

Then Srimati Radharani also appeared in the dream to confirm Gopinatha’s order. She told Jahnava-devi not to hesitate, for it was also Her desire to have a second Deity installed.

Then Radha and Gopinatha disappeared. Jahnava awoke and at once began to arrange for a larger Deity of Radha. That Deity was eventually installed on Gopinatha’s left side, as per His desire.

In this way, two Deities of Sri Radha stood beside Gopinatha, a situation that enhanced the Lord’s inconceivable beauty and glory in the minds of the resident devotees and visiting pilgrims.

According to the book Bhaktamala, Jahnava-devi had the second Deity installed not as Radha, but as Radharani’s younger sister Ananga-manjari, which is Jahnava’s identity in Krsna’s pastimes.

In addition to Radha and Ananga-manjari, Gopinatha also enjoys the company of Lalita and Visakha in their deity forms. Thus, true to His name, He is the master and enjoyer of the gopis.

As Jayadeva Gosvami writes,

**“My dear friends, just see how Sri Krsna is enjoying the season of spring! With the gopis embracing each of His limbs, He is like amorous love personified.**

**“With His transcendental pastimes, He enlivens all the gopis and the entire creation. With His soft bluish-black arms and legs, which resemble blue lotus flowers, He has created a festival for Cupid.’’**

Jahnava-devi is the potency of Lord Nityananda. She was an itinerant preacher and, for some time, the most senior devotee among Gaudiya Vaisnavas, highly respected hy the likes of Jiva Gosvami.

**\* \* \* \***

South of Gopinatha’s temple is the samadhi of Ramananda Raya, one of Caitanya Mahaprahhu’s most intimate associates Offering our obeisances, we should respectfully call out to him.

One of Bhavananda Raya’s five sons, Ramananda Raya was the governor of the city of Vidyanagara on the river Godavari, the southernmost border of Maharaja Prataparudra’s kingdom.

Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya was well acquainted with Ramananda Raya. As Lord Caitanya was about to leave for His tour of South India, Sarvabhauma advised the Lord to meet Ramananda:

**“I did not recognise his greatness when I first met him, but now that You have saved me I know that no one in this world can compare to Ramananda in knowledge of Krsna’s amorous dealings.”**

Caitanya Mahaprabhu did meet with Ramananda Raya. Srila Prabhupada wrote a small book entitled Sri Ramananda-samvada based on their conversations as recorded in Sri Caitanya-caritamrta.

After their talks, just before Caitanya Mahaprabhu continued His tour of South India, He requested Ramananda Raya to retire from government service, move to Jagannatha Puri, and live with Him.

This request of the Lord was unique. Nowhere else in the Lord’s biographies does Lord Caitanya request one of His associates- even intimate ones like Gadadhara and Nitai — to come and live with Him.

Just the opposite! Lord Caitanya told Gadadhara Pandita a, lor exampie, to stay in Puri when the Lord went to Vrndavana. And He told Nityananda to stop coming to Puri, to stay in Bengal and preach.

But to Ramananda the Lord said, “Give up all material engagements and come to Jagannatha Puri… The two of us shall remain together there and happily pass our time discussing topics of Krsna.”

Ramananda Raya followed the instructions of the Lord. One can still see his home in Puri today, just oft the Grand Road. From there he would walk to the Gabhira every evening to meet Lord Caitanya.

Lord Caitanya once told Bhavananda Raya, “You formerly appeared as Pandu, and your five sons appeared as the five Pandavas.... Ramananda Raya and I are one, although our bodies are different.”

Ramananda Raya’s spiritual identity is quite complex. Kavi-karnapura, after saying he is both the gopa Arjuna and the Pandava Arjuna, concludes that he is a combination of three personalities:

“Ramananda Raya is the incarnation of Lalita-gopi, Arjuniya-gopi, and Pandava Arjuna.” Srila Prabhupada also adds, “In the opinion of others he was an incarnation of Visakha-devi.”

The general opinion of Gaudiya Vaisnavas — as proclaimed by Srila Prabhupada — is that Ramananda Raya is an incarnation of Visakha-devi as well as the Pandava Arjuna and Arjuniya-gopi.

Needless to say, Ramananda Raya was a very confidential associate of Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Along with Svarupa Damodara, he would spend most of the night discussing Krsna with Lord Caitanya.

Ramananda Raya was such a great devotional scholar that he would quote a variety of verses from Srimad-Bhagavatam and the works of many different Vaisnavas to enhance the Lord’s ecstatic moods.

Srila Prabhupada compared Ramananda Raya’s service, assisting Lord Caitanya in His feelings of separation, to Subala’s assisting Krsna in His dealings with Srimati Radharani.

The Lord considered Ramananda Raya, a grhastha, equal in renunciation to Sanatana Gosvami, a sannyasi, because they were both materially detached and fully engaged in Sri Krsna’s service.

Sometimes Ramananda Raya, as he instructed the temple dancing girls how to enact his drama Jagannatha-vallabha, would massage, bathe, and dress the girls, absorbed in the mood of a maidservant.

About this, Caitanya Mahaprabhu commented, “Although I am a sannyasi, My mind is sometimes perturbed when I see a woman. But Ramananda Raya is greater than Me, for he is always undisturbed —

**“Even when he touches a woman.” Srila Prabhupada adds, “Only Ramananda Raya was endowed with the prerogative to touch a woman in this way; no one should imitate him.”**

Ramananda Raya was an accomplished playwright. His most famous drama was the Jagannatha-vallabha-nataka. While visiting his birthplace I saw in a glass case another, allegedly unpublished script.

Caitanya Mahaprabhu once took Ramananda to visit Rupa Gosvami in order to judge the poetic and literary excellence of Rupa’s Lalita-madhava and Vidagdha-madhava. The Raya was highly pleased.

Lord Caitanya would often quote from Ramananda’s work, verses such as this lament of Radha: “Our Krsna does not realise what We have suffered from injuries inflicted in the course of loving affairs.

“We are actually misused by love because love does not know where to strike and where not to strike. Even Cupid does not know of Our very much weakened condition. What should I tell anyone?

“No one can understand another’s difficulties. Our life is actually not under Our control, for youth will remain for two or three days and soon he finished. In this condition, what will he Our destination?"

Ramananda Raya was about twenty years older than Sri Caitanya, yet after the Lord’s disappearance, Ramananda stayed in this world and endured being separated from the Lord with extreme difficulty.

It is said that Ramananda Raya and Sarvabhauma Bhattacarya would meet daily to reminisce about their days with Caitanya Mahaprabhu. There is no reliable date given for Ramananda’s disappearance.

Bhakti-ratnakara provides the last historical reference to Ramananda Raya. That reference is related to Srinivasa Thakura’s visit to Puri and his meeting with Lord Caitanya’s associates there.

All glories, all glories to Ramananda Raya, to whom Lord Caitanya gave the mission of expanding the knowledge of devotional mellows. Rupa Gosvami was Ramananda’s instrument in fulfilling this task.

Adjoining Ramananda Raya’s samadhi is the tomb of Bilvamangala Thakura, of whom I have already written in connection with Brahma-kunda, the place at which he performed bhajana.

While writing of Lord Caitanya, Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami says,

**“Lilasuka \[Bilvamangala Thakura\] was an ordinary human being, yet he developed many ecstatic symptoms in his body.’’**

How did Bilvamangala attain such spiritual perfection? Amongst his many qualifications, Bilvamangala Thakura had the level of determination that Krsna praises in Bhagavad-gita.

Bilvamangala Thakura was so determined to see Krsna that when his own eyes became an obstruction to his spiritual advancement he blinded himself. That is strong determination (drdha-vratah).

Krsna rewarded Bilvamangala Thakura for his determination by granting the saint direct vision of Himself, which assured Bilvamahgala’s entrance into the eternal spiritual world at death.

Krsnadasa Kaviraja Gosvami’s commentary on the final verses of Krsna-karnamrta takes the form of a dialogue hetween Bilvamangala and Krsna, who has just directly revealed Himself along with Radha.

Bilvamangala, mad with ecstasy from seeing the Lord, could not properly describe Him. He called the Lord an “indescribable mass of bliss.” Krsna replied, “I want to hear about that sweetness. Describe it!”

“I can only offer respect to that ocean of sweetness because it is beyond words,” Bilvamangala responded. Krsna continued to tease him, “If words cannot describe it, use your mind to envision it.”

Again Bilvamangala excused himself, “Your sweetness is beyond thought as much as it is beyond words.” Krsna replied, “If something cannot be expressed by words or thought how can it be perceived?”

The saint said, “Your sweetness reveals itself to those whose hearts are overcome with the moods of love for You that arise from having envisioned You, which in turn causes a longing to see You directly.”

Krsna changed the subject and asked, “By calling Me a mass of bliss are You saying I am the impersonal Brahman?” Lilasuka smiled, “No! You are the brilliant sapphire ornament of Gokula whom I adore.”
